I also reviewed the latest Claude session you attached. And the external landscape has moved fast. JURIG is already advertising itself as an Android-first autonomous reverse-engineering agent; Open-ReverseLab has 100+ MCP tools and an evidence-oriented analysis chain; agentic-malware-analysis already does structured multi-phase malware analysis with evidence/hypotheses; and the 2026 Malaika paper directly tackles evidence-grounded agentic Android malware understanding.  So the audit absolutely needs to challenge KAVACH's differentiation rather than assuming the architecture is novel.

OWASP's current mobile guidance is also particularly relevant now: MASTG v2.0.0 and MASWE v1.0.0 were released in 2026, and OWASP explicitly emphasizes combining automated analysis with expert/manual review, plus static and dynamic analysis.  NIST's SSDF likewise explicitly recommends scoped security testing, fuzzing, root-cause analysis, and penetration testing for high-risk scenarios.

5. ATTACK THE ARTIFACTSTORE
======================================================================

Treat ArtifactStore as a security-critical subsystem.

Attempt to reason through and, where practical, test:

- same artifact ID + identical content
- same artifact ID + different content
- same artifact ID + altered metadata

- nonexistent artifact handle

- absolute path injection

- prefix-collision directories
- TOCTOU-style filesystem problems
- deletion between lookup and use
- partial writes
- DB succeeds / CAS fails
- CAS succeeds / DB fails
- process crash between storage and persistence
- duplicate writes
- concurrent writes
- restart
- stale in-memory state
- database corruption
- missing parent artifact
- cross-run parent artifact
- forged lineage
- modified stored bytes
- permissions changes
- filesystem replacement
- hardlink behavior
- copied staging behavior
- deterministic ZIP behavior
- content hash mismatch

Specifically determine whether "content addressed" and "immutable" are ACTUALLY guaranteed or merely intended.

- ignore failed tools;
- hide uncertainty.

Attempt adversarial examples.

Model says:
"Found suspicious C2 domain."

But no tool produced that domain.

Can the system accidentally accept it?

Model says:
"Evidence confirms persistence."

But the only input is an interpretation.

Can the hypothesis become CONFIRMED?

Tool returns malicious-looking text generated by an untrusted APK.

Can that text manipulate the agent?

Does the system distinguish data from instructions?

→ investigation
→ model
→ tools
→ artifacts
→ evidence
→ hypothesis
→ risk
→ report
→ verification

Then identify exactly which pieces are real, incomplete, mocked, fragile, or misleading.

A demo must NEVER fake:
- malicious findings
- evidence
- model identity
- tool execution
- ledger verification
- chain of custody
- persistence.

If a sample is benign, the report must say so.

======================================================================
31. AUDIT OUTPUT
======================================================================

At the end, produce the following sections in this exact order:

1. EXECUTIVE VERDICT
2. CURRENT SCORE /100
3. WHAT KAVACH ACTUALLY IS TODAY
4. WHAT KAVACH IS NOT YET
5. TOP 10 CRITICAL FINDINGS
6. SECURITY CRITICAL FINDINGS
7. CORRECTNESS / DATA-INTEGRITY FINDINGS
8. AGENT / MODEL FINDINGS
9. ARTIFACT / LEDGER / PROVENANCE FINDINGS
10. SANDBOX / EXECUTION FINDINGS
11. API / FRONTEND FINDINGS
12. TESTING / COVERAGE / FALSE-CONFIDENCE FINDINGS
13. REPRODUCIBILITY / SUPPLY-CHAIN FINDINGS
14. PERFORMANCE / SCALABILITY FINDINGS
15. COMPETITOR COMPARISON
16. NOVELTY / DIFFERENTIATION JUDGMENT
17. 10→100 ROADMAP
18. NEXT 10 ENGINEERING MOVES
19. DEMO-BLOCKING ISSUES
20. "WHAT I WOULD DELETE"
21. "WHAT I WOULD REWRITE"
22. "WHAT I WOULD KEEP"
23. "WHAT I WOULD BUILD NEXT"
24. 100/100 ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA

For each serious finding include:
- ID
- severity
- confidence
- file
- line/range
- technical explanation
- impact
- reproduction/test strategy
- recommended remediation
- dependencies/blockers.

======================================================================
32. CREATE AN AUDIT ARTIFACT
======================================================================

Write the complete audit to:

AUDIT/KAVACH_2.0_BRUTAL_AUDIT_2026-10-05.md

Also create a machine-readable findings file if practical:

AUDIT/KAVACH_2.0_FINDINGS_2026-10-05.json

Each finding should have a stable ID.

Do NOT modify production code merely to make the audit look better.

Do NOT quietly fix findings while auditing them.

The audit should represent the state that was actually inspected.

If you discover something so dangerous that running a test could damage the host or execute genuinely malicious code, stop that particular test and explain why.

Use harmless fixtures and safe simulations for adversarial cases.

======================================================================
33. EXECUTION RULES
======================================================================

Use your available tools aggressively.

You are expected to:
- inspect the filesystem;
- inspect git;
- inspect source;
- inspect configuration;
- run tests;
- run builds;
- run static checks;
- run targeted integration tests;
- inspect database schemas/migrations;
- inspect dependency resolution;
- inspect CI;
- search for security-sensitive patterns;
- compare implementation against architecture claims;
- research current public competitors.

Do not ask me for permission every few minutes.

Do the investigation yourself.

Do not stop after finding a few obvious issues.

Continue until you have high confidence that you understand the system.

When you cannot verify something:
mark it UNVERIFIED rather than guessing.

When documentation and code disagree:
CODE WINS.

When tests and runtime behavior disagree:
RUNTIME WINS.

When marketing claims and evidence disagree:
EVIDENCE WINS.

When uncertain:
be conservative.

======================================================================
34. FINAL QUESTION YOU MUST ANSWER
======================================================================

After everything above, answer this as honestly as possible:

"If I put KAVACH AI 2.0 in front of a very strong senior malware analyst, reverse engineer, application-security engineer, and AI-agent engineer tomorrow, exactly what would impress them, exactly what would make them skeptical, exactly what would get rejected, and exactly what would make them say 'this is genuinely serious engineering'?"

Do not flatter me.

Tell me what would happen.

One extra instruction I strongly recommend adding

At the very end, add this:

IMPORTANT: Do not optimize the audit for making me feel good or for making the project appear advanced.

Optimize for discovering embarrassing truths BEFORE someone else discovers them.

I would rather receive:
"this subsystem is fundamentally broken"

than:
"this is a strong foundation with room for improvement."

I would rather receive:
"this feature is mostly cosmetic"

than:
"this feature has good potential."

Assume the goal is not to win a demo.
The goal is to build something that survives hostile technical scrutiny.

That changes the psychology of the audit substantially.

Why I made this much harsher than a normal Claude audit

The current KAVACH work actually has some serious engineering pieces now. The latest session reports three commits covering durable ArtifactStore behavior, controller-level ArtifactStore integration, a Postgres hash-chained ledger, execution records, and evidence provenance. But the same session says the new service has no tests yet, FastAPI dependencies aren't yet in requirements.txt, the new Postgres lifecycle/trigger tests hadn't been run, and the native tool protocol and demo wiring were unfinished. 

So the danger now isn't “you haven't built enough.” It's false confidence: sophisticated-looking infrastructure whose actual end-to-end behavior hasn't been attacked hard enough.

And the ecosystem gives us a reason to be especially unforgiving. Open-ReverseLab already has a huge agent/tool surface and evidence-chain concept; JURIG explicitly targets autonomous Android reverse engineering; agentic-malware-analysis already has multi-phase evidence/hypothesis workflows; and Malaika is explicitly about auditable, evidence-grounded Android malware reasoning. 

So KAVACH's eventual “100/100” cannot simply be:

> AI + APK + JADX + autonomous agent.



That's already crowded.

The truly valuable question for Opus is:

> Does KAVACH actually provide a trustworthy, reproducible, security-bounded chain from hostile Android bytes → analysis actions → derived artifacts → evidence → hypothesis → conclusion?



That is the line I would make it attack relentlessly.

One more thing: OWASP's 2026 MASTG/MASWE updates make it worth explicitly forcing the audit to map KAVACH against current mobile-security methodology rather than an old checklist. 

And don't let Opus spend the entire two hours writing the report before testing. The prompt deliberately tells it to inspect and execute first, then write the report. That's the best use of that remaining weekly allowance

Use skills like caveman, ponytail, obsidian, graphify, code graph for ur help and sudo pass is 123@qwerty for any needs, dont be shy to use it.

<h1 align="center">🛡️ KAVACH AI (कवच AI) 2.0</h1>

<p align="center">
  <strong>Evidence-driven forensic investigation platform for Android applications</strong>
</p>

---

> [!WARNING]
> **Status: demo-grade vertical slice, not production-ready.**
> An APK upload runs through triage, an autonomous tool-calling investigation, a hash-chained ledger and a
> deterministic report (`backend/service/`). Bind the service to localhost only: in-process parsers still
> read hostile bytes inside the KAVACH process.
> The KAVACH 1.x application (FastAPI + Gemini + MobSF/Frida dashboard) has been retired from this branch;
> its final state is preserved at the git tag [`v1-final`](https://github.com/RamNarra/KAVACH-AI/tree/v1-final).

---

## What exists today

| Component | Location | State |
|---|---|---|
| Static analysis DAG orchestrator | `backend/orchestration/`, `backend/analyzers/` | Androguard, APKiD, manifest, MobSF, Quark, YARA-X analyzers run concurrently with per-stage timeouts |
| Deterministic risk policy & report | `backend/risk/policy_engine.py`, `backend/intelligence/deterministic_reporter.py` | YAML-driven policy, deterministic report generation |
| Canonical persistence | `backend/persistence/`, `backend/alembic/` | SQLAlchemy 2 async + PostgreSQL: samples, runs, stage executions, evidence, relationships, risk, reports, artifacts |
| Investigation controller | `backend/agent/` | Multi-turn tool-calling loop with supervisor budget, repetition, diminishing-returns and frontier-gate checks; claim-specific hypothesis adjudication (`agent/adjudication.py`) |
| Model gateway | `backend/intelligence/` | Provider abstraction over an OpenAI-compatible `llama-server`; requires an explicitly resolved model profile; mock provider for tests |
| Execution broker & sandbox | `backend/execution/`, `backend/workbench/` | Policy-escalated isolation tiers; bubblewrap-hardened out-of-process execution that fails closed |
| Forensic tools | `backend/tools/` | Container repair, payload decoding, string/xref search, JADX, ELF symbols. Separate investigation and self-repair registries; schema-invalid calls are rejected before execution; artifacts are addressed only by opaque `artifact://` handles; JADX and readelf run through the ExecutionBroker sandbox; the model sees only a sanitized, size-bounded `ToolObservation` marked as untrusted data |
| InvestigationService + API | `backend/service/` | Upload an APK, follow the investigation over SSE, read artifacts, verify the ledger, fetch the report. Run rows move QUEUED, RUNNING, then one terminal state (COMPLETED, COMPLETED_NO_FINDINGS, PARTIAL, FAILED, TIMEOUT, INTERRUPTED) that matches the ledger's RUN_FINALIZED event. Every read comes from PostgreSQL and the ArtifactStore, so runs survive a restart |
| ArtifactStore | `backend/storage/` | Content-addressed blobs, PostgreSQL descriptors, run-scoped opaque `artifact://` handles, lineage (parent, execution, tool call) |
| InvestigationLedger | `backend/ledger/` | Append-only, hash-chained run events in PostgreSQL (UPDATE/DELETE refused by trigger), independent chain verifier, execution records for every tool call; evidence without source artifact, execution and tool call is rejected |
| Runtime toolchain | `backend/system/toolchain.py` | Resolves llama-server, model, JADX, APKTool, YARA, Java, adb from `KAVACH_*_PATH`, then `PATH`, then appliance paths |

## Known limitations (tracked for the next phases)

- In-process tools (container inspection and repair, payload decoding, string/xref search) parse hostile bytes inside the KAVACH process; only JADX and readelf run in the bubblewrap sandbox.
- The served model's weights are not verified against the configured profile at run time.
- Static analysis only: no dynamic execution of samples.
- Context control is simple: when a prompt would overflow the model window, the oldest tool observations are elided (their full results stay in the ledger). There is no relevance-ranked context assembly.
- The legacy text tool-call parser (`LlamaCppReasoningProvider(legacy_text_tool_calls=True)`) is kept for compatibility only; the investigation path uses native `tool_calls` and `role=tool` messages.
- `backend/evals/agent_eval.py` is diagnostic telemetry and not suitable for model comparison.
- `evals/run_evals.py` scores hand-authored responses; it is a synthetic fixture, **not** an empirical measurement of KAVACH.
- `frontend/` targets the retired 1.x API and is unsupported; the 2.0 demo console is `backend/service/console/index.html`.
- The appliance build is only partially reproducible: base image, llama.cpp images, JADX, APKTool and the GGUF are pinned by digest/SHA-256, but Python requirements use version floors (no lockfile) and apt packages are unpinned.

## Demo

```bash
cd backend
.venv/bin/alembic upgrade head                                  # PostgreSQL schema
.venv/bin/python -m service.demo_fixture /tmp/kavach_demo.apk  # harmless synthetic sample
KAVACH_MODEL_ID=qwen3.5-4b KAVACH_LLAMA_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8092/v1 \
  .venv/bin/uvicorn service.main:create_production_app --factory --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000
```

Start `llama-server` with the Qwen3.5-4B GGUF on port 8092 first, then open http://127.0.0.1:8000 and upload
an APK. The demo fixture is a benign APK-shaped ZIP with one fake ZIP encryption flag; it is not malware.

## Directory layout

```
backend/
  agent/           investigation controller, supervisor, prompt builder
  analyzers/       static analyzer plugins
  domain/          canonical domain models (evidence, run, investigation, trace, ...)
  execution/       ExecutionBroker, policy, backend resolver, bwrap backend
  intelligence/    model gateway, providers, model profiles, deterministic reporter
  orchestration/   async static-analysis DAG
  persistence/     database engine, ORM models, persistence service
  system/          host doctor, resource governor, runtime toolchain resolution
  risk/            deterministic risk policy engine
  service/         InvestigationService, HTTP API, demo console, demo fixture
  storage/         content-addressed ArtifactStore
  ledger/          hash-chained InvestigationLedger and execution records
  tools/           forensic tool registry and tools
  workbench/       sandbox executor, filesystem domains, policy
  rules/           YARA rules and behaviour catalog
deploy/            appliance Dockerfile, compose (PostgreSQL + llama-server) and dev override
docs/              design notes (provenance requirements)
frontend/          unsupported (1.x UI)
scripts/           setup and verification scripts
```

## Development

```bash
./scripts/setup.sh                              # create backend venv, install requirements (Python only;
                                                # --with-frontend also installs the unsupported 1.x UI)
(cd backend && venv/bin/python -m system.doctor) # check host toolchain (bwrap, jadx, models, ...)
./scripts/verify_all.sh                         # run the backend verification gate
```

`verify_all.sh` reports each suite separately:

| Suite | Needs | Default |
|---|---|---|
| UNIT, CONTRACT | Python deps only (synthetic, benign fixtures) | required |
| SECURITY | bubblewrap >= 0.12 in an approved root (`/opt/kavach/runtime/bin`, `/usr/bin`, ...) | required |
| INTEGRATION | PostgreSQL (`POSTGRES_*`), Docker Compose CLI; includes `alembic check` (zero drift, unknown tables flagged) | required |
| REPAIR | git (developer self-repair worktree tooling; disposable clone) | required |
| ACCEPTANCE | private APK corpus via `KAVACH_ACCEPTANCE_FIXTURES` (never committed) | NOT RUN unless set |
| MODEL | `KAVACH_RUN_MODEL_SUITE=1`, `KAVACH_LLAMA_SERVER_PATH`, `KAVACH_MODEL_PATH` (optional `KAVACH_MODEL_SHA256`, `KAVACH_PROBE_GPU_LAYERS`, `KAVACH_PROBE_DEVICE`) | NOT RUN unless set |

A skipped test in a required suite fails the gate; it is never counted as a pass.

### Appliance compose

`deploy/docker-compose.appliance.yml` keeps PostgreSQL and llama-server on an internal network with no
published ports, and reads the database password from a Compose secret
(`deploy/secrets/postgres_password` or `KAVACH_POSTGRES_PASSWORD_FILE`). For host access during
development add `-f deploy/docker-compose.dev.yml` (binds 127.0.0.1 only). A one-shot `model-verify`
service checks the GGUF against `KAVACH_MODEL_SHA256` before either llama-server starts.

### Phase 1 Invariants & Boundaries

- **Hostile-byte parser boundary**: `InvestigationService` must not be exposed as a production-facing API until in-process hostile-byte parsers have explicit size/resource bounds or have been moved behind the execution boundary.
- **Model identity verification**: Verifying that the actually loaded and served inference model SHA-256 matches the configured model profile digest is a mandatory admission gate before admitting runs.

    42	def build_production_service() -> InvestigationService:
    43	    data_dir = Path(os.environ.get("KAVACH_DATA_DIR") or DEFAULT_DATA_DIR).resolve()
    44	    store = ArtifactStore.create_filesystem_store(data_dir / "artifacts", persistence_service=PersistenceService)
    45	    ledger = InvestigationLedger(PostgresLedgerStore())
    46	    # Resolve the model profile now: an unknown or forbidden profile fails at startup, not mid-run.
    47	    ModelManager.create_gateway()
    48	
    49	    def controller_factory(run_store: ArtifactStore, run_ledger: InvestigationLedger) -> InvestigationController:
    50	        return InvestigationController(
    51	            ModelManager.create_gateway(), build_demo_tool_registry(),
    52	            artifact_store=run_store, ledger=run_ledger,
    53	        )
    54	
    55	    return InvestigationService(
    56	        store=store,
    57	        ledger=ledger,
    58	        runs=PostgresRunRepository(),
    59	        controller_factory=controller_factory,
    60	        work_root=data_dir / "work",
    61	        max_turns=int(os.environ.get("KAVACH_MAX_TURNS", "8")),
    62	        max_seconds=float(os.environ.get("KAVACH_MAX_SECONDS", "900")),
    63	        active_tools=demo_tools_available(),

    65	
    66	
    67	def demo_tools_available() -> Set[str]:
    68	    """Offer run_jadx only when a JADX binary resolves on this host; the other demo tools are in-process."""
    69	    tools = set(DEMO_TOOL_NAMES)
    70	    if try_resolve_tool("jadx") is None:
    71	        tools.discard("run_jadx")
    72	    return tools
    73	
    74	
    75	def create_production_app() -> FastAPI:
    76	    return create_app(build_production_service(), on_shutdown=dispose_async_engine)
    77	"""
    78	HTTP API for the InvestigationService (FastAPI).
    79	
    80	Demo boundary, not a production API: bind it to localhost. In-process parsers still read hostile
    81	APK bytes inside this process (docs/provenance-requirements.md, Phase 1 gate 1).
    82	
    83	    POST /investigations                      upload an APK (multipart field "file")
    84	    GET  /investigations                      recent runs
    85	    GET  /investigations/{run_id}             status and event counts
    86	    GET  /investigations/{run_id}/events      ledger events (?after=<sequence>)
    87	    GET  /investigations/{run_id}/stream      ledger events as server-sent events until RUN_FINALIZED

    93	
    94	from __future__ import annotations
    95	import asyncio
    96	import json
    97	from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
    98	from pathlib import Path
    99	from typing import Any, AsyncIterator, Awaitable, Callable, Dict, Optional
   100	
   101	from fastapi import FastAPI, File, HTTPException, Query, UploadFile
   102	from fastapi.responses import FileResponse, StreamingResponse
   103	
   104	from ledger.models import EventType
   105	from service.investigations import IngestionFailed, InvestigationService, RunNotFound, UploadRejected
   106	
   107	CONSOLE_HTML = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "console" / "index.html"
   108	STREAM_POLL_SECONDS = 0.5
   109	
   110	
   111	def _artifact_json(a: Any) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   112	    return {
   113	        "artifact_id": a.artifact_id, "handle": a.handle, "artifact_type": a.artifact_type, "sha256": a.sha256,
   114	        "size_bytes": a.size_bytes, "mime_type": a.mime_type, "storage_uri": a.storage_uri,
   115	        "parent_artifact_ids": list(a.parent_artifact_ids), "produced_by_execution_id": a.produced_by_execution_id,
   116	        "produced_by_tool_call_id": a.produced_by_tool_call_id, "producer": a.producer,
   117	        "producer_version": a.producer_version, "created_at": a.created_at.isoformat(),
   118	    }
   119	
   120	
   121	def create_app(
   122	    service: InvestigationService,
   123	    on_shutdown: Optional[Callable[[], Awaitable[None]]] = None,
   124	) -> FastAPI:
   125	    @asynccontextmanager
   126	    async def lifespan(_: FastAPI) -> AsyncIterator[None]:
   127	        # Close runs a previous process left open, then cancel our own in-flight runs on shutdown
   128	        # (each one records INTERRUPTED in the ledger and on its run row).
   129	        await service.recover_interrupted()
   130	        try:
   131	            yield
   132	        finally:
   133	            await service.shutdown()
   134	            if on_shutdown is not None:
   135	                await on_shutdown()
   136	
   137	    app = FastAPI(title="KAVACH AI 2.0 InvestigationService", version="2.0.0-demo", lifespan=lifespan)
   138	    app.state.service = service
   139	
   140	    async def _guard(coro):
   141	        try:
   142	            return await coro
   143	        except RunNotFound:
   144	            raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="investigation not found") from None
   145	
   146	    @app.post("/investigations", status_code=202)
   147	    async def create_investigation(file: UploadFile = File(...)) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   148	        content = await file.read(service.max_upload_bytes + 1)
   149	        try:
   150	            record = await service.create(content, file.filename)
   151	        except UploadRejected as e:
   152	            raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=str(e)) from None
   153	        except IngestionFailed as e:
   154	            raise HTTPException(status_code=500, detail={"run_id": e.run_id, "status": "FAILED", "error": str(e)}) from None
   155	        return record.model_dump(mode="json")
   156	
   157	    @app.get("/investigations")
   158	    async def list_investigations(limit: int = Query(50, ge=1, le=500)):
   159	        return await service.list(limit)
   160	
   161	    @app.get("/investigations/{run_id}")
   162	    async def get_investigation(run_id: str):
   163	        return await _guard(service.get(run_id))
   164	
   165	    @app.get("/investigations/{run_id}/events")
   166	    async def get_events(run_id: str, after: int = Query(0, ge=0)):
   167	        events = await _guard(service.events(run_id, after))
   168	        return [e.model_dump(mode="json") for e in events]
   169	
   170	    @app.get("/investigations/{run_id}/stream")
   171	    async def stream_events(run_id: str, after: int = Query(0, ge=0)):
   172	        await _guard(service.get(run_id))
   173	
   174	        async def gen():
   175	            cursor = after
   176	            while True:
   177	                events = await service.events(run_id, cursor)
   178	                for e in events:
   179	                    cursor = e.sequence
   180	                    yield f"id: {e.sequence}\nevent: ledger\ndata: {json.dumps(e.model_dump(mode='json'))}\n\n"
   181	                    if e.event_type == EventType.RUN_FINALIZED:
   182	                        yield "event: end\ndata: {}\n\n"
   183	                        return
   184	                await asyncio.sleep(STREAM_POLL_SECONDS)
   185	
   186	        return StreamingResponse(gen(), media_type="text/event-stream", headers={"Cache-Control": "no-cache"})
   187	
   188	    @app.get("/investigations/{run_id}/artifacts")
   189	    async def get_artifacts(run_id: str):
   190	        return [_artifact_json(a) for a in await _guard(service.artifacts(run_id))]
   191	
   192	    @app.get("/investigations/{run_id}/verify")
   193	    async def verify(run_id: str):
   194	        v = await _guard(service.verify(run_id))
   195	        return {"valid": v.valid, "event_count": v.event_count, "head_hash": v.head_hash, "errors": v.errors}
   196	
   197	    @app.get("/investigations/{run_id}/report")
   198	    async def report(run_id: str):
   199	        return await _guard(service.report(run_id))
   200	
   201	    @app.get("/", include_in_schema=False)
   202	    async def console():
   203	        return FileResponse(CONSOLE_HTML, media_type="text/html")
   204	
   205	    return app

--- [Step 10 | THINKING] ---
I notice create_investigation loads the entire upload into memory, and storage_uri being exposed in the API could leak host path info. The streaming logic also lacks a client disconnect check and could loop forever if a run never finalizes or if service.events throws, plus `_guard` only applies on the initial get. I want to keep reading through investigations.py, runs.py, and the report module to confirm these issues.

[TOOL RESULT | SUCCESS]
     1	"""
     2	InvestigationService: ingest an APK, run triage and the agent investigation, and serve canonical state.
     3	
     4	Every read (status, events, artifacts, report) comes from persisted state: the run repository,
     5	the InvestigationLedger and the ArtifactStore. Nothing is read from controller memory, so a process
     6	restart does not erase an investigation. A run interrupted by a restart is closed in the ledger as
     7	INTERRUPTED on the next start.
     8	"""
     9	
    10	from __future__ import annotations
    11	import asyncio
    12	import hashlib
    13	import logging
    14	import re
    15	import uuid
    16	from datetime import datetime, timezone
    17	from pathlib import Path
    18	from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, List, Optional, Set
    19	
    20	from agent.controller import ORIGINAL_ARTIFACT_ID, InvestigationController
    21	from domain.execution_state import RUN_TERMINAL_STATES, ExecutionState, parse_run_state
    22	from ledger import EventType, InvestigationLedger
    23	from ledger.chain import LedgerVerification, verify_chain
    24	from ledger.models import LedgerEvent
    25	from service.report import build_report, run_status
    26	from service.runs import RunRecord, RunRepository
    27	from service.triage import run_triage
    28	from storage.store import ArtifactStore
    29	from tools.base import sanitize_model_text
    30	from tools.registry import DEMO_TOOL_NAMES
    31	
    32	logger = logging.getLogger("kavach.service.investigations")
    33	
    34	DEFAULT_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES = 64 * 1024 * 1024
    35	DEFAULT_MAX_SECONDS = 900.0
    36	ZIP_MAGIC = b"PK\x03\x04"
    37	
    38	# Demo toolset: the strongest stable tools only (Phase 1 demo sprint, Task 8).
    39	DEMO_TOOLS = DEMO_TOOL_NAMES
    40	
    41	class UploadRejected(ValueError):
    42	    """The uploaded file is not an acceptable APK (size or container signature)."""
    43	
    44	
    45	class RunNotFound(KeyError):
    46	    pass
    47	
    48	
    49	class IngestionFailed(RuntimeError):
    50	    """The APK could not be stored; the run was closed as FAILED."""
    51	
    52	    def __init__(self, run_id: str, error: str) -> None:
    53	        super().__init__(error)
    54	        self.run_id = run_id
    55	
    56	
    57	ControllerFactory = Callable[[ArtifactStore, InvestigationLedger], InvestigationController]
    58	
    59	
    60	def _safe_file_name(name: Optional[str]) -> str:
    61	    base = Path(name or "sample.apk").name
    62	    return re.sub(r"[^A-Za-z0-9._-]", "_", base)[:128] or "sample.apk"
    63	
    64	
    65	class InvestigationService:
    66	    def __init__(
    67	        self,
    68	        store: ArtifactStore,
    69	        ledger: InvestigationLedger,
    70	        runs: RunRepository,
    71	        controller_factory: ControllerFactory,
    72	        work_root: Path | str,
    73	        max_turns: int = 12,
    74	        max_upload_bytes: int = DEFAULT_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES,
    75	        max_seconds: float = DEFAULT_MAX_SECONDS,
    76	        active_tools: Optional[Set[str]] = None,
    77	    ) -> None:
    78	        self.store = store
    79	        self.ledger = ledger
    80	        self.runs = runs
    81	        self.controller_factory = controller_factory
    82	        self.work_root = Path(work_root).resolve()
    83	        self.max_turns = max_turns
    84	        self.max_upload_bytes = max_upload_bytes
    85	        self.max_seconds = max_seconds
    86	        self.active_tools = set(active_tools or DEMO_TOOLS)
    87	        # One investigation at a time: a single local model server serves every run.
    88	        self._slot = asyncio.Semaphore(1)
    89	        self._tasks: Dict[str, asyncio.Task] = {}
    90	
    91	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ writes
    92	
    93	    async def create(self, content: bytes, file_name: Optional[str]) -> RunRecord:
    94	        """Create the run (QUEUED), ingest the APK into the ArtifactStore, then start its investigation.
    95	
    96	        If ingestion fails the run is closed as FAILED in the ledger and on the run row before the
    97	        error propagates, so no run is left open without a task.
    98	        """
    99	        if not content or len(content) > self.max_upload_bytes:
   100	            raise UploadRejected(f"APK must be between 1 byte and {self.max_upload_bytes} bytes")
   101	        if not content.startswith(ZIP_MAGIC):
   102	            raise UploadRejected("File is not a ZIP/APK container (missing PK\\x03\\x04 signature)")
   103	
   104	        run_id = f"run_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:16]}"
   105	        record = await self.runs.create(RunRecord(
   106	            run_id=run_id,
   107	            sample_id=f"sample_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:16]}",
   108	            sample_sha256=hashlib.sha256(content).hexdigest(),
   109	            file_name=_safe_file_name(file_name),
   110	            size_bytes=len(content),
   111	            created_at=datetime.now(timezone.utc),
   112	        ))
   113	        try:
   114	            await self.store.put_bytes(
   115	                run_id=run_id, content=content, artifact_type="ORIGINAL_APK", producer="ingestion",
   116	                artifact_id=ORIGINAL_ARTIFACT_ID, mime_type="application/vnd.android.package-archive",
   117	                metadata={"file_suffix": ".apk"},
   118	            )
   119	        except Exception as e:
   120	            logger.exception(f"Ingestion of {run_id} failed")
   121	            error = sanitize_model_text(f"ingestion failed: {type(e).__name__}: {e}", 500)
   122	            # If closing the run also fails, recover_interrupted() closes it on the next start.
   123	            await self._close(run_id, ExecutionState.FAILED, error)
   124	            raise IngestionFailed(run_id, error) from e
   125	        self._tasks[run_id] = asyncio.create_task(self._execute(record), name=f"investigation-{run_id}")
   126	        return record
   127	
   128	    async def wait(self, run_id: str) -> None:
   129	        """Wait for an in-flight investigation (tests and graceful shutdown)."""
   130	        task = self._tasks.get(run_id)
   131	        if task is not None:
   132	            await asyncio.shield(task)
   133	
   134	    async def shutdown(self) -> None:
   135	        """Cancel in-flight investigations; each one closes itself as INTERRUPTED."""
   136	        tasks = list(self._tasks.values())
   137	        for task in tasks:
   138	            task.cancel()
   139	        await asyncio.gather(*tasks, return_exceptions=True)
   140	
   141	    async def _execute(self, record: RunRecord) -> None:
   142	        run_id = record.run_id
   143	        try:
   144	            async with self._slot:
   145	                await self.runs.mark_running(run_id)
   146	                try:
   147	                    await asyncio.wait_for(self._investigate(record), timeout=self.max_seconds)
   148	                except asyncio.TimeoutError:
   149	                    logger.error(f"Investigation {run_id} exceeded {self.max_seconds}s")
   150	                    await self._close(run_id, ExecutionState.TIMEOUT, f"investigation exceeded {self.max_seconds} seconds")
   151	                    return
   152	                # The controller recorded RUN_FINALIZED; _close mirrors exactly that state onto the run row
   153	                # (and only records FAILED itself if the controller returned without finalizing).
   154	                await self._close(run_id, ExecutionState.FAILED, "controller returned without RUN_FINALIZED")
   155	        except asyncio.CancelledError:
   156	            await self._close(run_id, ExecutionState.INTERRUPTED, "investigation cancelled by service shutdown")
   157	            raise
   158	        except Exception as e:  # recorded, never swallowed: the ledger and run row show the failure
   159	            logger.exception(f"Investigation {run_id} failed")
   160	            await self._close(run_id, ExecutionState.FAILED, sanitize_model_text(f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}", 500))
   161	        finally:
   162	            self._tasks.pop(run_id, None)
   163	
   164	    async def _investigate(self, record: RunRecord) -> None:
   165	        run_id = record.run_id
   166	        work_dir = self.work_root / run_id
   167	        original = await self.store.materialize(ORIGINAL_ARTIFACT_ID, run_id, work_dir / "triage")
   168	        triage = await run_triage(run_id, record.sample_id, original, work_dir / "triage")
   169	        controller = self.controller_factory(self.store, self.ledger)
   170	        await controller.run_investigation(
   171	            run_id=run_id,
   172	            sample_id=record.sample_id,
   173	            apk_path=str(original),
   174	            baseline_evidence=triage.baseline_evidence,
   175	            work_dir=str(work_dir / "investigation"),
   176	            initial_hypotheses=triage.hypotheses,
   177	            max_turns=self.max_turns,
   178	            initial_active_tools=self.active_tools,
   179	            sample_identity=triage.identity,
   180	        )
   181	
   182	    async def _close(self, run_id: str, state: ExecutionState, error: Optional[str]) -> None:
   183	        """Close a run in the ledger (unless already finalized) and mirror the ledger's state onto the row."""
   184	        events = await self.ledger.events(run_id)
   185	        final = next((e for e in events if e.event_type == EventType.RUN_FINALIZED), None)
   186	        if final is None:
   187	            final = await self.ledger.append(run_id, EventType.RUN_FINALIZED, {"status": state.value, "error": error},
   188	                                             causation_id=events[-1].event_id if events else None)
   189	        await self.runs.mark_finished(run_id, final.payload["status"], final.payload.get("error"))
   190	
   191	    async def recover_interrupted(self) -> List[str]:
   192	        """Close runs left open by a previous process (their task died with it).
   193	
   194	        A run whose ledger is finalized but whose row is not (crash between the two writes) gets its
   195	        row repaired to the ledger's state; it is not reported as interrupted.
   196	        """
   197	        closed = []
   198	        for record in await self.runs.list(limit=500):
   199	            if record.run_id in self._tasks or parse_run_state(record.status) in RUN_TERMINAL_STATES:
   200	                continue
   201	            events = await self.ledger.events(record.run_id)
   202	            finalized = any(e.event_type == EventType.RUN_FINALIZED for e in events)
   203	            await self._close(record.run_id, ExecutionState.INTERRUPTED, "process restarted before the run finished")
   204	            if not finalized:
   205	                closed.append(record.run_id)
   206	        return closed
   207	
   208	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ reads (canonical state only)
   209	
   210	    async def _record(self, run_id: str) -> RunRecord:
   211	        record = await self.runs.get(run_id)
   212	        if record is None:
   213	            raise RunNotFound(run_id)
   214	        return record
   215	
   216	    async def get(self, run_id: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   217	        record = await self._record(run_id)
   218	        events = await self.ledger.events(run_id)
   219	        counts: Dict[str, int] = {}
   220	        for e in events:
   221	            counts[e.event_type.value] = counts.get(e.event_type.value, 0) + 1
   222	        return {
   223	            **record.model_dump(mode="json"),
   224	            "status": run_status(events, record.status),
   225	            "event_count": len(events),
   226	            "event_counts": counts,
   227	            "in_flight": run_id in self._tasks,
   228	        }
   229	
   230	    async def list(self, limit: int = 50) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
   231	        out = []
   232	        for record in await self.runs.list(limit=limit):
   233	            events = await self.ledger.events(record.run_id)
   234	            out.append({**record.model_dump(mode="json"), "status": run_status(events, record.status),
   235	                        "event_count": len(events)})
   236	        return out
   237	
   238	    async def events(self, run_id: str, after: int = 0) -> List[LedgerEvent]:
   239	        await self._record(run_id)
   240	        return [e for e in await self.ledger.events(run_id) if e.sequence > after]
   241	
   242	    async def artifacts(self, run_id: str) -> List[Any]:
   243	        await self._record(run_id)
   244	        return await self.store.list_artifacts_for_run(run_id)
   245	
   246	    async def verify(self, run_id: str) -> LedgerVerification:
   247	        await self._record(run_id)
   248	        return verify_chain(await self.ledger.events(run_id))
   249	
   250	    async def report(self, run_id: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   251	        record = await self._record(run_id)
   252	        events = await self.ledger.events(run_id)
   253	        artifacts = await self.store.list_artifacts_for_run(run_id)
   254	        return build_report(record, events, artifacts, verify_chain(events))
   255	
   256	"""
   257	Run identity records (sample + run rows). Investigation state itself lives in the ledger and the
   258	ArtifactStore; this repository answers "which runs exist, which sample does each analyze, and which
   259	lifecycle state did the run reach".
   260	
   261	Run lifecycle: QUEUED (accepted, waiting for the investigation slot) -> RUNNING (slot acquired) ->
   262	one terminal state of domain.execution_state.RUN_TERMINAL_STATES. The terminal state is the same
   263	value the RUN_FINALIZED ledger event records; the ledger stays canonical.
   264	"""
   265	
   266	from __future__ import annotations
   267	from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
   268	from datetime import datetime, timezone
   269	from typing import Dict, List, Optional
   270	
   271	from pydantic import BaseModel
   272	
   273	from domain.execution_state import RUN_TERMINAL_STATES, ExecutionState, parse_run_state
   274	
   275	
   276	class RunRecord(BaseModel):
   277	    run_id: str
   278	    sample_id: str
   279	    sample_sha256: str
   280	    file_name: str
   281	    size_bytes: int
   282	    created_at: datetime
   283	    status: str = ExecutionState.QUEUED.value
   284	    error: Optional[str] = None
   285	
   286	
   287	class RunNotRecorded(LookupError):
   288	    """A lifecycle update named a run the repository does not hold."""
   289	
   290	
   291	def _terminal(status: ExecutionState | str) -> ExecutionState:
   292	    state = parse_run_state(status)
   293	    if state not in RUN_TERMINAL_STATES:
   294	        raise ValueError(f"{state.value} is not a terminal run state")
   295	    return state
   296	
   297	
   298	class RunRepository(ABC):
   299	    @abstractmethod
   300	    async def create(self, record: RunRecord) -> RunRecord:
   301	        """Persist a new run in state QUEUED."""
   302	
   303	    @abstractmethod
   304	    async def get(self, run_id: str) -> Optional[RunRecord]:
   305	        ...
   306	
   307	    @abstractmethod
   308	    async def list(self, limit: int = 50) -> List[RunRecord]:
   309	        ...
   310	
   311	    @abstractmethod
   312	    async def mark_running(self, run_id: str) -> None:
   313	        """QUEUED -> RUNNING once the investigation slot is acquired."""
   314	
   315	    @abstractmethod
   316	    async def mark_finished(self, run_id: str, status: ExecutionState | str, error: Optional[str] = None) -> None:
   317	        """Record the terminal state. Raises ValueError for a non-terminal or unknown state."""
   318	
   319	
   320	class MemoryRunRepository(RunRepository):
   321	    def __init__(self) -> None:
   322	        self._runs: Dict[str, RunRecord] = {}
   323	
   324	    async def create(self, record: RunRecord) -> RunRecord:
   325	        record = record.model_copy(update={"status": ExecutionState.QUEUED.value, "error": None})
   326	        self._runs[record.run_id] = record
   327	        return record
   328	
   329	    async def get(self, run_id: str) -> Optional[RunRecord]:
   330	        return self._runs.get(run_id)
   331	
   332	    async def list(self, limit: int = 50) -> List[RunRecord]:
   333	        return sorted(self._runs.values(), key=lambda r: r.created_at, reverse=True)[:limit]
   334	
   335	    async def mark_running(self, run_id: str) -> None:
   336	        self._update(run_id, ExecutionState.RUNNING.value, None)
   337	
   338	    async def mark_finished(self, run_id: str, status: ExecutionState | str, error: Optional[str] = None) -> None:
   339	        self._update(run_id, _terminal(status).value, error)
   340	
   341	    def _update(self, run_id: str, status: str, error: Optional[str]) -> None:
   342	        if run_id not in self._runs:
   343	            raise RunNotRecorded(run_id)
   344	        self._runs[run_id] = self._runs[run_id].model_copy(update={"status": status, "error": error})
   345	
   346	
   347	class PostgresRunRepository(RunRepository):
   348	    async def create(self, record: RunRecord) -> RunRecord:
   349	        from domain.run import AnalysisProfile, AnalysisRun
   350	        from domain.sample import Sample
   351	        from persistence.service import PersistenceService
   352	
   353	        sample = await PersistenceService.create_sample(Sample(
   354	            id=record.sample_id, sha256=record.sample_sha256, file_size_bytes=record.size_bytes,
   355	            file_name=record.file_name, storage_path=f"cas://{record.sample_sha256}", ingested_at=record.created_at,
   356	        ))
   357	        # create_sample deduplicates by SHA-256: a re-uploaded sample reuses the existing sample row.
   358	        record = record.model_copy(update={"sample_id": sample.id, "status": ExecutionState.QUEUED.value, "error": None})
   359	        await PersistenceService.create_analysis_run(AnalysisRun(
   360	            id=record.run_id, sample_id=sample.id, status=ExecutionState.QUEUED,
   361	            profile=AnalysisProfile.FORENSIC_TRIAGE, started_at=record.created_at,
   362	        ))
   363	        return record
   364	
   365	    async def get(self, run_id: str) -> Optional[RunRecord]:
   366	        rows = await self._select(run_id=run_id, limit=1)
   367	        return rows[0] if rows else None
   368	
   369	    async def list(self, limit: int = 50) -> List[RunRecord]:
   370	        return await self._select(limit=limit)
   371	
   372	    async def mark_running(self, run_id: str) -> None:
   373	        await self._update(run_id, ExecutionState.RUNNING, None, finished=False)
   374	
   375	    async def mark_finished(self, run_id: str, status: ExecutionState | str, error: Optional[str] = None) -> None:
   376	        await self._update(run_id, _terminal(status), error, finished=True)
   377	
   378	    async def _update(self, run_id: str, state: ExecutionState, error: Optional[str], finished: bool) -> None:
   379	        from sqlalchemy import select
   380	        from persistence.database import get_session
   381	        from persistence.models import AnalysisRunModel
   382	
   383	        async with get_session() as session:
   384	            run = (await session.execute(
   385	                select(AnalysisRunModel).where(AnalysisRunModel.id == run_id)
   386	            )).scalar_one_or_none()
   387	            if run is None:
   388	                raise RunNotRecorded(run_id)
   389	            run.status = state.value
   390	            run.error_message = error
   391	            if finished:
   392	                now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
   393	                run.completed_at = now
   394	                if run.started_at:
   395	                    run.duration_seconds = max(0.0, (now - run.started_at).total_seconds())
   396	
   397	    async def _select(self, run_id: Optional[str] = None, limit: int = 50) -> List[RunRecord]:
   398	        from sqlalchemy import select
   399	        from persistence.database import get_session
   400	        from persistence.models import AnalysisRunModel, SampleModel
   401	
   402	        stmt = (
   403	            select(AnalysisRunModel, SampleModel)
   404	            .join(SampleModel, SampleModel.id == AnalysisRunModel.sample_id)
   405	            .where(AnalysisRunModel.profile == "FORENSIC_TRIAGE")
   406	            .order_by(AnalysisRunModel.started_at.desc())
   407	            .limit(limit)
   408	        )
   409	        if run_id is not None:
   410	            stmt = stmt.where(AnalysisRunModel.id == run_id)
   411	        async with get_session() as session:
   412	            rows = (await session.execute(stmt)).all()
   413	        return [
   414	            RunRecord(
   415	                run_id=run.id, sample_id=sample.id, sample_sha256=sample.sha256, file_name=sample.file_name,
   416	                size_bytes=sample.file_size_bytes,
   417	                created_at=run.started_at or datetime.now(timezone.utc),
   418	                status=run.status, error=run.error_message,
   419	            )
   420	            for run, sample in rows
   421	        ]

--- [Step 12 | THINKING] ---
I'm noting that recover_interrupted only checks the 500 most-recent runs, so older non-terminal runs restricted to the FORENSIC_TRIAGE profile never get recovered. I'm also flagging a potential misclassification issue in `_execute`: an internal asyncio.TimeoutError from the controller itself (not just the wait_for timeout) could get incorrectly recorded as TIMEOUT.

A bigger concern is concurrency across multiple uvicorn workers — since semaphores and task tracking are per-process, a rolling restart or second instance could cause recover_interrupted in one worker to mark another worker's in-flight runs INTERRUPTED, raising questions about whether the ledger rejects appends after finalization. I'm also considering a non-atomic race in `_close` between controller finalize and timeout-close that could produce duplicate RUN_FINALIZED events, and looking at how `create()` deduplicates sample rows by sha256 and reuses the first uploaded file's name.

I want to check whether the controller swallows CancelledError during wait_for timeouts, since that could prevent cancellation from actually stopping the investigation, and then move on to examining report.py, triage.py, and the demo fixture.



--- [Step 13 | TOOL INVOCATION: Bash] ---
{
  "command": "cat -n service/report.py service/triage.py service/demo_fixture.py",
  "description": "Read report, triage, demo fixture"
}

[TOOL RESULT | SUCCESS]
     1	"""
     2	Deterministic investigation report, built only from persisted state: the run record, the ledger
     3	events and the ArtifactStore descriptors. The same inputs always yield the same report, so the
     4	report is reproducible from the ledger at any time and is never a second source of truth.
     5	"""
     6	
     7	from __future__ import annotations
     8	from datetime import datetime
     9	from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional
    10	
    11	from domain.evidence import Evidence
    12	from ledger.chain import LedgerVerification
    13	from ledger.models import EventType, LedgerEvent
    14	from risk.policy_engine import PolicyEngine
    15	
    16	REPORT_SCHEMA_VERSION = "kavach-demo-report/1"
    17	COMPLETE_STATUSES = {"COMPLETED", "COMPLETED_NO_FINDINGS"}
    18	
    19	STANDING_LIMITATIONS = [
    20	    "Static analysis only: no dynamic execution of the sample was performed.",
    21	    "In-process parsers (container, strings, xrefs, payload decoding) read hostile bytes inside the KAVACH process; "
    22	    "only external tools (JADX, readelf) run in the bubblewrap sandbox.",
    23	    "The served model's weights are not verified against the configured profile at run time.",
    24	]
    25	
    26	
    27	def _first(events: List[LedgerEvent], event_type: EventType) -> Optional[LedgerEvent]:
    28	    return next((e for e in events if e.event_type == event_type), None)
    29	
    30	
    31	def run_status(events: List[LedgerEvent], row_status: Optional[str] = None) -> str:
    32	    """The RUN_FINALIZED state when the ledger is closed, else the run row's QUEUED/RUNNING state."""
    33	    final = _first(events, EventType.RUN_FINALIZED)
    34	    if final is not None:
    35	        return str(final.payload.get("status", "UNKNOWN"))
    36	    if row_status in ("QUEUED", "RUNNING"):
    37	        return row_status
    38	    return "RUNNING" if events else "QUEUED"
    39	
    40	
    41	def _hypotheses(events: List[LedgerEvent], finished: bool) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    42	    hyps: Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]] = {}
    43	    for e in events:
    44	        if e.event_type == EventType.HYPOTHESIS_PROPOSED:
    45	            p = e.payload
    46	            hyps[p["hypothesis_id"]] = {
    47	                "hypothesis_id": p["hypothesis_id"], "claim_type": p["claim_type"], "claim_summary": p["claim_summary"],
    48	                "target": p.get("target"), "status": p.get("status", "PROPOSED"), "proposed_seq": e.sequence,
    49	                "resolved_by_evidence_id": None, "resolved_seq": None,
    50	            }
    51	        elif e.event_type == EventType.HYPOTHESIS_TRANSITIONED and e.payload.get("hypothesis_id") in hyps:
    52	            h = hyps[e.payload["hypothesis_id"]]
    53	            h["status"] = e.payload["new_status"]
    54	            h["resolved_by_evidence_id"] = e.payload.get("promoted_evidence_id")
    55	            h["resolved_seq"] = e.sequence
    56	    for h in hyps.values():
    57	        if finished and h["status"] not in ("CONFIRMED", "REFUTED"):
    58	            h["status"] = "UNRESOLVED"
    59	    return sorted(hyps.values(), key=lambda h: h["proposed_seq"])
    60	
    61	
    62	def _timeline_entry(e: LedgerEvent) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    63	    p = e.payload
    64	    summary = {
    65	        EventType.RUN_STARTED: lambda: f"Run started with model {p.get('model_id')}",
    66	        EventType.ARTIFACT_INGESTED: lambda: f"Ingested {p.get('handle')} ({p.get('size_bytes')} bytes)",
    67	        EventType.MODEL_CALLED: lambda: (f"Model turn {p.get('turn_index')}: "
    68	                                         + (", ".join(c["name"] for c in p.get("tool_calls") or []) or "no tool calls")
    69	                                         + (f" [error: {p['error']}]" if p.get("error") else "")),
    70	        EventType.TOOL_CALLED: lambda: f"Tool call {p.get('tool')} ({p.get('tool_call_id')})",
    71	        EventType.EXECUTION_COMPLETED: lambda: (f"{p.get('tool_id')} finished {p.get('status')} "
    72	                                                f"[{p.get('backend')}, isolation {p.get('effective_isolation')}]"),
    73	        EventType.ARTIFACT_CREATED: lambda: f"Derived artifact {p.get('handle')} from {', '.join(p.get('parent_artifact_ids') or []) or 'none'}",
    74	        EventType.EVIDENCE_ASSERTED: lambda: f"Evidence {p.get('type')} ({p.get('severity')})" + (" [baseline]" if p.get("baseline") else ""),
    75	        EventType.HYPOTHESIS_PROPOSED: lambda: f"Hypothesis proposed: {p.get('claim_type')}",
    76	        EventType.HYPOTHESIS_TRANSITIONED: lambda: f"Hypothesis {p.get('claim_type')}: {p.get('previous_status')} -> {p.get('new_status')}",
    77	        EventType.SUPERVISOR_DECISION: lambda: f"Supervisor {p.get('decision_type')}: {p.get('action')}",
    78	        EventType.RUN_FINALIZED: lambda: f"Run finalized: {p.get('status')}",
    79	    }[e.event_type]()
    80	    return {"sequence": e.sequence, "event_id": e.event_id, "causation_id": e.causation_id,
    81	            "event_type": e.event_type.value, "timestamp": e.timestamp.isoformat(), "summary": summary}
    82	
    83	
    84	def build_report(run: Any, events: List[LedgerEvent], artifacts: List[Any], verification: LedgerVerification) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    85	    started = _first(events, EventType.RUN_STARTED)
    86	    final = _first(events, EventType.RUN_FINALIZED)
    87	    status = run_status(events, getattr(run, "status", None))
    88	    finished = final is not None
    89	    start_payload = started.payload if started else {}
    90	
    91	    executions = {e.payload["execution_id"]: e.payload for e in events if e.event_type == EventType.EXECUTION_COMPLETED}
    92	    evidence_events = [e for e in events if e.event_type == EventType.EVIDENCE_ASSERTED]
    93	    evidence_rows = []
    94	    evidence_objects = []
    95	    for e in evidence_events:
    96	        p = e.payload
    97	        execution = executions.get(p.get("source_execution_id") or "")
    98	        evidence_rows.append({
    99	            "evidence_id": p["id"], "type": p["type"], "title": p["title"], "category": p["category"],
   100	            "severity": p["severity"], "confidence": p["confidence"], "origin": p["origin"],
   101	            "baseline": p.get("baseline", False), "source_artifact_id": p.get("source_artifact_id"),
   102	            "source_execution_id": p.get("source_execution_id"), "source_tool_call_id": p.get("source_tool_call_id"),
   103	            "source_tool": execution["tool_id"] if execution else p.get("analyzer"), "ledger_sequence": e.sequence,
   104	        })
   105	        evidence_objects.append(Evidence.model_validate({k: v for k, v in p.items() if k != "baseline"}))
   106	
   107	    hypotheses = _hypotheses(events, finished)
   108	    risk = PolicyEngine().evaluate(run.run_id, evidence_objects)
   109	    key_findings = [f"{h['claim_type']} confirmed: {h['claim_summary']}" for h in hypotheses if h["status"] == "CONFIRMED"]
   110	    key_findings += [f"{r['title']} ({r['severity']})" for r in evidence_rows if r["severity"] in ("CRITICAL", "HIGH")]
   111	
   112	    limitations = list(STANDING_LIMITATIONS)
   113	    limitations += [f"Hypothesis {h['claim_type']} remained unresolved." for h in hypotheses if h["status"] == "UNRESOLVED"]
   114	    if status not in COMPLETE_STATUSES:
   115	        limitations.append(f"Investigation status is {status}; the report reflects the ledger so far.")
   116	    for e in events:
   117	        if e.event_type == EventType.SUPERVISOR_DECISION and e.payload.get("decision_type") == "TOOL_CALL_REJECTED":
   118	            d = e.payload.get("details") or {}
   119	            limitations.append(f"Tool call {d.get('tool')} was refused ({d.get('error')}).")
   120	
   121	    duration = None
   122	    if started and final:
   123	        duration = round((final.timestamp - started.timestamp).total_seconds(), 3)
   124	
   125	    return {
   126	        "schema_version": REPORT_SCHEMA_VERSION,
   127	        "title": "KAVACH AI 2.0 - Autonomous Android Malware Investigation",
   128	        "sample": {
   129	            "sha256": run.sample_sha256, "file_name": run.file_name, "size_bytes": run.size_bytes,
   130	            "package_name": (start_payload.get("sample_identity") or {}).get("package_name"),
   131	            "version_name": (start_payload.get("sample_identity") or {}).get("version_name"),
   132	        },
   133	        "investigation": {
   134	            "run_id": run.run_id, "status": status,
   135	            "model_id": start_payload.get("model_id"), "provider_type": start_payload.get("provider_type"),
   136	            "turn_count": sum(1 for e in events if e.event_type == EventType.MODEL_CALLED),
   137	            "tool_calls": sum(1 for e in events if e.event_type == EventType.TOOL_CALLED),
   138	            "started_at": started.timestamp.isoformat() if started else None,
   139	            "finished_at": final.timestamp.isoformat() if final else None,
   140	            "duration_seconds": duration,
   141	        },
   142	        "evidence": evidence_rows,
   143	        "hypotheses": hypotheses,
   144	        "artifacts": [{
   145	            "artifact_id": a.artifact_id, "handle": a.handle, "artifact_type": a.artifact_type, "sha256": a.sha256,
   146	            "size_bytes": a.size_bytes, "parent_artifact_ids": list(a.parent_artifact_ids),
   147	            "produced_by_execution_id": a.produced_by_execution_id, "produced_by_tool_call_id": a.produced_by_tool_call_id,
   148	            "producer": a.producer,
   149	        } for a in artifacts],
   150	        "executions": [{
   151	            k: v for k, v in rec.items() if k in ("execution_id", "tool_id", "tool_version", "backend", "effective_isolation",
   152	                                                 "network_policy", "status", "exit_code", "executable_sha256",
   153	                                                 "input_artifacts", "output_artifacts", "tool_call_id")
   154	        } for rec in executions.values()],
   155	        "timeline": [_timeline_entry(e) for e in events],
   156	        "final": {
   157	            "risk_level": risk.threat_level.value if hasattr(risk.threat_level, "value") else str(risk.threat_level),
   158	            "risk_score": risk.composite_score,
   159	            "risk_summary": risk.summary_verdict,
   160	            "key_findings": key_findings,
   161	            "limitations": limitations,
   162	            "ledger_verification": {
   163	                "valid": verification.valid, "event_count": verification.event_count,
   164	                "head_hash": verification.head_hash, "errors": verification.errors,
   165	            },
   166	        },
   167	    }
   168	"""
   169	Deterministic static triage that runs before the agent.
   170	
   171	Triage extracts the sample identity, records baseline evidence (manifest permissions) and proposes
   172	hypotheses from container anomalies. Triage only proposes: a hypothesis becomes CONFIRMED later,
   173	and only when an agent tool produces evidence that satisfies its claim predicate.
   174	"""
   175	
   176	from __future__ import annotations
   177	import math
   178	import re
   179	import zipfile
   180	from collections import Counter
   181	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
   182	from pathlib import Path
   183	from typing import Any, Dict, List
   184	
   185	from analyzers.results import AnalysisContext
   186	from analyzers.static.manifest import ManifestPlugin
   187	from domain.evidence import Evidence
   188	from domain.investigation import EpistemicStatus, Hypothesis
   189	
   190	ORIGINAL_HANDLE = "artifact://original_apk"
   191	_PAYLOAD_MAGIC = {b"dex\n": "DEX", b"\x7fELF": "ELF"}
   192	_STANDARD_CODE = ("classes", "lib/")
   193	MAX_ENTRY_SCAN_BYTES = 8 * 1024 * 1024
   194	
   195	
   196	@dataclass
   197	class TriageResult:
   198	    identity: Dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
   199	    baseline_evidence: List[Evidence] = field(default_factory=list)
   200	    hypotheses: List[Hypothesis] = field(default_factory=list)
   201	    observations: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
   202	
   203	
   204	def _safe_entry_name(name: str) -> str:
   205	    """ZIP entry names are attacker-controlled and reach the model prompt: keep a short, inert token."""
   206	    cleaned = re.sub(r"[^A-Za-z0-9._/-]", "_", name)
   207	    return cleaned if len(cleaned) <= 64 else cleaned[:61] + "..."
   208	
   209	
   210	def _entropy(data: bytes) -> float:
   211	    if not data:
   212	        return 0.0
   213	    counts = Counter(data)
   214	    n = len(data)
   215	    return -sum(c / n * math.log2(c / n) for c in counts.values())
   216	
   217	
   218	def _hypothesis(claim_type: str, summary: str) -> Hypothesis:
   219	    return Hypothesis(
   220	        investigation_id="triage",
   221	        target_identifier=ORIGINAL_HANDLE,
   222	        claim_type=claim_type,
   223	        claim_summary=summary,
   224	        epistemic_status=EpistemicStatus.PROPOSED,
   225	    )
   226	
   227	
   228	async def run_triage(run_id: str, sample_id: str, apk_path: Path, work_dir: Path) -> TriageResult:
   229	    result = TriageResult()
   230	
   231	    manifest = await ManifestPlugin().analyze(AnalysisContext(
   232	        run_id=run_id, sample_id=sample_id, apk_path=str(apk_path), work_dir=str(work_dir),
   233	    ))
   234	    result.identity = dict(manifest.raw_output or {})
   235	    # Baseline evidence: only findings above INFO, so the agent's context stays focused.
   236	    result.baseline_evidence = [ev for ev in manifest.evidence if ev.severity.value != "INFO"]
   237	    result.observations.extend(manifest.warnings)
   238	
   239	    fake_encrypted: List[str] = []
   240	    payload_entries: List[str] = []
   241	    try:
   242	        with zipfile.ZipFile(apk_path) as zf:
   243	            for info in zf.infolist():
   244	                if info.flag_bits & 0x1:
   245	                    fake_encrypted.append(_safe_entry_name(info.filename))
   246	                    continue
   247	                if info.filename.startswith(_STANDARD_CODE) or info.file_size == 0:
   248	                    continue
   249	                if info.file_size > MAX_ENTRY_SCAN_BYTES:
   250	                    continue
   251	                data = zf.read(info)
   252	                magic = _PAYLOAD_MAGIC.get(data[:4])
   253	                if magic or (info.filename.startswith("assets/") and info.file_size >= 512 and _entropy(data) > 7.2):
   254	                    payload_entries.append(f"{_safe_entry_name(info.filename)} ({magic or 'high entropy'})")
   255	    except zipfile.BadZipFile as e:
   256	        result.observations.append(f"container could not be parsed as ZIP: {e}")
   257	        result.hypotheses.append(_hypothesis("ZIP_CORRUPTION", "The APK container is malformed; repair may recover it."))
   258	        return result
   259	
   260	    if fake_encrypted:
   261	        result.observations.append(f"entries flagged encrypted without real encryption: {', '.join(fake_encrypted[:5])}")
   262	        result.hypotheses.append(_hypothesis(
   263	            "CONTAINER_EVASION",
   264	            f"ZIP entries carry encryption flags ({', '.join(fake_encrypted[:3])}) that block standard parsers.",
   265	        ))
   266	    if payload_entries:
   267	        result.observations.append(f"embedded payload candidates: {', '.join(payload_entries[:5])}")
   268	        result.hypotheses.append(_hypothesis(
   269	            "HIDDEN_PAYLOAD",
   270	            f"Non-standard entries look like embedded code: {', '.join(payload_entries[:3])}.",
   271	        ))
   272	    return result
   273	"""
   274	Harmless synthetic demo sample.
   275	
   276	The APK-shaped ZIP holds marker strings, a DEX-magic stub and one asset whose ZIP headers carry the
   277	"encrypted" flag without any real encryption. That container anomaly is a known parser-confusion
   278	trick and nothing else: the sample has no code, no behavior and no malicious content. It exists to
   279	exercise the pipeline (triage, tool calls, a derived artifact, evidence, a hypothesis transition,
   280	the report and ledger verification). Never present it as real malware.
   281	
   282	    .venv/bin/python -m service.demo_fixture /tmp/kavach_demo.apk
   283	"""
   284	
   285	from __future__ import annotations
   286	import struct
   287	import sys
   288	import zipfile
   289	from pathlib import Path
   290	from typing import List
   291	
   292	FLAGGED_ENTRY = "assets/notes.txt"
   293	PACKAGE_MARKER = "com.kavach.demo.fixture"
   294	DEMO_PERMISSIONS = ["android.permission.INTERNET", "android.permission.READ_SMS"]
   295	
   296	
   297	def _flag_entry_encrypted(buf: bytearray, entry_name: str) -> None:
   298	    """Set general-purpose bit 0 on the named entry's local and central headers; data stays plain."""
   299	    name = entry_name.encode()
   300	    for sig, flag_offset, name_offset in ((b"PK\x03\x04", 6, 30), (b"PK\x01\x02", 8, 46)):
   301	        start = 0
   302	        while (idx := buf.find(sig, start)) != -1:
   303	            if buf[idx + name_offset: idx + name_offset + len(name)] == name:
   304	                (flag,) = struct.unpack_from("<H", buf, idx + flag_offset)
   305	                struct.pack_into("<H", buf, idx + flag_offset, flag | 0x0001)
   306	            start = idx + 4
   307	
   308	
   309	ANDROID_NS = "http://schemas.android.com/apk/res/android"
   310	_NO_INDEX = 0xFFFFFFFF
   311	_TYPE_STRING = 0x03
   312	
   313	
   314	def _string_pool(strings: List[str]) -> bytes:
   315	    """UTF-16 ResStringPool chunk."""
   316	    body = bytearray()
   317	    offsets = []
   318	    for value in strings:
   319	        offsets.append(len(body))
   320	        encoded = value.encode("utf-16-le")
   321	        body += struct.pack("<H", len(value)) + encoded + b"\x00\x00"
   322	    while len(body) % 4:
   323	        body += b"\x00"
   324	    header_size = 28
   325	    strings_start = header_size + 4 * len(strings)
   326	    size = strings_start + len(body)
   327	    return (struct.pack("<HHIIIIII", 0x0001, header_size, size, len(strings), 0, 0, strings_start, 0)
   328	            + struct.pack(f"<{len(strings)}I", *offsets) + bytes(body))
   329	
   330	
   331	def binary_manifest(package: str, permissions: List[str]) -> bytes:
   332	    """Minimal binary AXML AndroidManifest: <manifest package=...> with <uses-permission> children."""
   333	    strings = ["android", ANDROID_NS, "manifest", "package", package, "uses-permission", "name", *permissions]
   334	    idx = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(strings)}
   335	
   336	    def chunk(chunk_type: int, body: bytes) -> bytes:
   337	        return struct.pack("<HHI", chunk_type, 16, 8 + len(body)) + body  # body starts with line + comment
   338	
   339	    def node(extra: bytes) -> bytes:  # line number + no comment, then the node extension
   340	        return struct.pack("<II", 1, _NO_INDEX) + extra
   341	
   342	    def start(name: str, attrs: List[tuple]) -> bytes:
   343	        ext = struct.pack("<IIHHHHHH", _NO_INDEX, idx[name], 0x14, 0x14, len(attrs), 0, 0, 0)
   344	        for ns, attr, value in attrs:
   345	            ext += struct.pack("<IIIHBBI", ns, idx[attr], idx[value], 8, 0, _TYPE_STRING, idx[value])
   346	        return chunk(0x0102, node(ext))
   347	
   348	    def end(name: str) -> bytes:
   349	        return chunk(0x0103, node(struct.pack("<II", _NO_INDEX, idx[name])))
   350	
   351	    ns_ext = struct.pack("<II", idx["android"], idx[ANDROID_NS])
   352	    xml = chunk(0x0100, node(ns_ext))
   353	    xml += start("manifest", [(_NO_INDEX, "package", package)])
   354	    for perm in permissions:
   355	        xml += start("uses-permission", [(idx[ANDROID_NS], "name", perm)]) + end("uses-permission")
   356	    xml += end("manifest") + chunk(0x0101, node(ns_ext))
   357	    body = _string_pool(strings) + xml
   358	    return struct.pack("<HHI", 0x0003, 8, 8 + len(body)) + body
   359	
   360	
   361	def build_demo_apk(path: Path | str) -> Path:
   362	    """Write the deterministic demo sample to path and return it."""
   363	    path = Path(path)
   364	    with zipfile.ZipFile(path, "w", compression=zipfile.ZIP_STORED) as zf:
   365	        zf.writestr("AndroidManifest.xml", binary_manifest(PACKAGE_MARKER, DEMO_PERMISSIONS))
   366	        zf.writestr("classes.dex", b"dex\n035\x00" + b"\x00" * 64 + b"Lcom/kavach/demo/fixture/MainActivity;")
   367	        zf.writestr(FLAGGED_ENTRY, b"KAVACH demo fixture. Harmless synthetic sample for pipeline demonstration.\n")
   368	        zf.writestr("res/values/strings.xml", b"<resources><string name='app_name'>KAVACH Demo</string></resources>")
   369	    buf = bytearray(path.read_bytes())
   370	    _flag_entry_encrypted(buf, FLAGGED_ENTRY)
   371	    path.write_bytes(bytes(buf))
   372	    return path
   373	
   374	
   375	if __name__ == "__main__":
   376	    if len(sys.argv) != 2:
   377	        sys.exit("usage: python -m service.demo_fixture <output.apk>")
   378	    print(build_demo_apk(sys.argv[1]))

--- [Step 14 | THINKING] ---
I'm looking at a potential issue where there's no limit on the number of ZIP entries processed—iterating over a zip file with a million entries could cause memory or time exhaustion. I'm also considering that the declared file size in a ZIP header is attacker-controlled, and checking how Python's zipfile module handles reads against that declared size versus the actual decompressed data and CRC verification.

I'm realizing a more serious problem: the triage logic does blocking zipfile reads synchronously, even though it's called from async code, which would freeze the entire event loop—meaning all other requests and SSE streams would hang, and the timeout mechanism couldn't even fire since the loop itself is blocked. That's a solid finding worth flagging, and I want to check whether the manifest plugin has a similar synchronous blocking issue with androguard.

I also notice the entropy calculation on 8MB of data using pure Python's Counter is probably acceptable performance-wise, and there's a smaller honesty issue where any encrypted flag bit is being labeled as "fake encryption" without actually verifying real encryption, which overstates what was observed versus what's actually being checked.

Now I'm looking at report generation issues: key findings might surface baseline permissions like READ_SMS as HIGH severity even for benign samples, so I need to check whether the demo fixture's risk score correctly reflects that it's benign and whether the hard-coded report title is a problem. I also spot a dictionary lookup on event type that could throw a KeyError for any event type not explicitly mapped, a Pydantic validation call on ledger payloads that could 500 on malformed data, reports being generated with computed risk scores even when ledger verification fails, and run_status silently taking the first RUN_FINALIZED event if duplicates exist. The ingestion storage path looks fine, so now I'm moving on to review the domain and ledger code.

     7	from ledger.store import InvestigationLedger, LedgerStore, MemoryLedgerStore, PostgresLedgerStore

    25	"""
    26	
    27	from __future__ import annotations
    28	from datetime import datetime
    29	from enum import Enum
    30	from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional
    31	
    32	from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
    33	
    34	
    35	class EventType(str, Enum):
    36	    RUN_STARTED = "RUN_STARTED"
    37	    ARTIFACT_INGESTED = "ARTIFACT_INGESTED"
    38	    MODEL_CALLED = "MODEL_CALLED"
    39	    TOOL_CALLED = "TOOL_CALLED"
    40	    EXECUTION_COMPLETED = "EXECUTION_COMPLETED"
    41	    ARTIFACT_CREATED = "ARTIFACT_CREATED"
    42	    EVIDENCE_ASSERTED = "EVIDENCE_ASSERTED"
    43	    HYPOTHESIS_PROPOSED = "HYPOTHESIS_PROPOSED"
    44	    HYPOTHESIS_TRANSITIONED = "HYPOTHESIS_TRANSITIONED"
    45	    SUPERVISOR_DECISION = "SUPERVISOR_DECISION"
