    97	                res = await client.get(
    98	                    health_url,
    99	                    headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {self.api_key}"},
   100	                )
   101	                return res.status_code == 200
   102	        except Exception:
   103	            return False
   104	
   105	    async def chat_complete(
   106	        self,
   107	        messages: List[Dict[str, Any]],
   108	        tools: Optional[List[Dict[str, Any]]] = None,
   109	        tool_choice: Optional[str | Dict[str, Any]] = None,
   110	        response_format: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
   111	        temperature: float = 0.1,
   112	        max_tokens: int = 2048,
   113	        reasoning_budget_tokens: Optional[int] = None,
   114	        cache_prompt: bool = True,
   115	    ) -> ProviderResponse:
   116	        start_time = time.time()
   117	        url = f"{self.base_url}/chat/completions"
   118	
   119	        payload: Dict[str, Any] = {
   120	            "messages": messages,
   121	            "temperature": temperature,
   122	            "max_tokens": max_tokens,
   123	            "stream": False,
   124	            "cache_prompt": cache_prompt,
   125	        }
   126	
   127	        if reasoning_budget_tokens is not None:
   128	            payload["reasoning_budget_tokens"] = reasoning_budget_tokens
   129	            if reasoning_budget_tokens == 0:
   130	                payload["reasoning_effort"] = "none"
   131	
   132	        if tools:
   133	            payload["tools"] = tools
   134	            if tool_choice:
   135	                payload["tool_choice"] = tool_choice
   136	            else:
   137	                payload["tool_choice"] = "auto"
   138	
   139	        if response_format:
   140	            payload["response_format"] = response_format
   141	
   142	        headers = {
   143	            "Content-Type": "application/json",
   144	            "Authorization": f"Bearer {self.api_key}",
   145	        }
   146	
   147	        async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=self.timeout, transport=self._transport) as client:
   148	            try:
   149	                resp = await client.post(url, json=payload, headers=headers)
   150	                resp.raise_for_status()
   151	                data = resp.json()
   152	            except httpx.HTTPStatusError as e:
   153	                logger.error(f"Llama-server HTTP error {e.response.status_code}: {e.response.text}")
   154	                raise RuntimeError(f"Llama-server returned error {e.response.status_code}: {e.response.text}") from e
   155	            except httpx.RequestError as e:
   156	                logger.error(f"Llama-server connection error: {e}")
   157	                raise ConnectionError(f"Could not connect to llama-server at {url}: {e}") from e
   158	
   159	        duration = round(time.time() - start_time, 3)
   160	
   161	        choices = data.get("choices", [])
   162	        timings = data.get("timings", {})
   163	        usage = data.get("usage", {})
   164	        if not choices:
   165	            return ProviderResponse(
   166	                content="",
   167	                model=data.get("model", "unknown"),
   168	                usage=usage,
   169	                timings=timings,
   170	                duration_seconds=duration,
   171	                finish_reason="stop",
   172	            )
   173	
   174	        choice = choices[0]
   175	        message = choice.get("message", {})
   176	        raw_content = message.get("content")
   177	        reasoning_content = message.get("reasoning_content")
   178	        finish_reason = choice.get("finish_reason")
   179	
   180	        parsed_tool_calls: List[ToolCall] = []
   181	
   182	        # 1. Native OpenAI-style tool calls
   183	        raw_tools = message.get("tool_calls", [])
   184	        for idx, t in enumerate(raw_tools):
   185	            fn = t.get("function", {})
   186	            fn_name = fn.get("name", "")
   187	            raw_args = fn.get("arguments", "{}")
   188	            if isinstance(raw_args, str):
   189	                try:
   190	                    args_dict = json.loads(raw_args)
   191	                except Exception:
   192	                    args_dict = {"raw": raw_args}
   193	            elif isinstance(raw_args, dict):
   194	                args_dict = raw_args
   195	            else:
   196	                args_dict = {}
   197	
   198	            parsed_tool_calls.append(
   199	                ToolCall(
   200	                    id=t.get("id", f"call_{idx}_{int(time.time())}"),
   201	                    name=fn_name,
   202	                    arguments=args_dict,
   203	                )
   204	            )
   205	
   206	        # 2. Legacy compatibility only (never in the native path): tool calls written into message text.
   207	        if self.legacy_text_tool_calls and not parsed_tool_calls and raw_content:
   208	            parsed_tool_calls.extend(self._extract_fallback_tool_calls(raw_content))
   209	
   210	        # 3. Parse structured JSON data if expected or present
   211	        structured_data: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None
   212	        if raw_content:
   213	            structured_data = self._extract_structured_json(raw_content)
   214	
   215	        return ProviderResponse(
   216	            content=raw_content,
   217	            tool_calls=parsed_tool_calls,
   218	            structured_data=structured_data,
   219	            model=data.get("model", "llama-cpp"),
   220	            usage=usage,
   221	            timings=timings,
   222	            reasoning_content=reasoning_content,
   223	            duration_seconds=duration,
   224	            finish_reason=finish_reason,
   225	        )
   226	
   227	    @staticmethod
   228	    def _extract_fallback_tool_calls(text: str) -> List[ToolCall]:
   229	        """Legacy compatibility: extract tool calls a model wrote as XML tags or fenced JSON in its text.
   230	
   231	        Used only when the provider was built with legacy_text_tool_calls=True.
   232	        """
   233	        calls: List[ToolCall] = []
   234	        # Pattern 1: <tool_call>{"name": ..., "arguments": ...}</tool_call>
   235	        pattern = r"<tool_call>[\s\r\n]*(\{.*?\})[\s\r\n]*</tool_call>"
   236	        matches = re.findall(pattern, text, re.DOTALL)
   237	        for idx, m in enumerate(matches):
   238	            try:
   239	                obj = json.loads(m.strip())
   240	                name = obj.get("name") or obj.get("tool")
   241	                args = obj.get("arguments") or obj.get("parameters") or {}
   242	                if name:
   243	                    calls.append(ToolCall(id=f"fallback_call_{idx}", name=name, arguments=args))
   244	            except Exception:
   245	                continue
   246	
   247	        # Pattern 2: ```json\n{"tool": "name", ...}\n```
   248	        if not calls:
   249	            json_blocks = re.findall(r"```(?:json)?\s*(\{.*?\})\s*```", text, re.DOTALL)
   250	            for idx, block in enumerate(json_blocks):
   251	                try:
   252	                    obj = json.loads(block.strip())
   253	                    name = obj.get("tool") or obj.get("name")
   254	                    args = obj.get("arguments") or obj.get("args") or {k: v for k, v in obj.items() if k not in ("tool", "name")}
   255	                    if name and isinstance(name, str):
   256	                        calls.append(ToolCall(id=f"block_call_{idx}", name=name, arguments=args))
   257	                except Exception:
   258	                    continue
   259	
   260	        return calls
   261	
   262	    def _extract_structured_json(self, text: str) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
   263	        """Safely parse JSON from LLM text (handling markdown wrappers)."""
   264	        cleaned = text.strip()
   265	        # Direct JSON
   266	        if cleaned.startswith("{") and cleaned.endswith("}"):
   267	            try:
   268	                return json.loads(cleaned)
   269	            except Exception:
   270	                pass
   271	        # Markdown fenced JSON
   272	        m = re.search(r"```(?:json)?\s*(\{.*?\})\s*```", cleaned, re.DOTALL)
   273	        if m:
   274	            try:
   275	                return json.loads(m.group(1).strip())
   276	            except Exception:
   277	                pass
   278	        return None
   279	
   280	
   281	class MockReasoningProvider(ReasoningProvider):
   282	    """
   283	    Deterministic Mock Provider for unit testing and offline execution.
   284	    Can be scripted with predetermined responses or call generators.
   285	    """
   286	
   287	    provider_type = ProviderType.MOCK
   288	
   289	    def __init__(self, responses: Optional[List[ProviderResponse]] = None) -> None:
   290	        self.responses = responses or []
   291	        self._index = 0
   292	        self.call_history: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
   293	
   294	    async def is_available(self) -> bool:
   295	        return True
   296	
   297	    def queue_response(self, response: ProviderResponse) -> None:
   298	        self.responses.append(response)
   299	
   300	    async def chat_complete(
   301	        self,
   302	        messages: List[Dict[str, Any]],
   303	        tools: Optional[List[Dict[str, Any]]] = None,
   304	        tool_choice: Optional[str | Dict[str, Any]] = None,
   305	        response_format: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
   306	        temperature: float = 0.1,
   307	        max_tokens: int = 2048,
   308	        reasoning_budget_tokens: Optional[int] = None,
   309	        cache_prompt: bool = True,
   310	        **kwargs: Any,
   311	    ) -> ProviderResponse:
   312	        self.call_history.append({
   313	            "messages": messages,
   314	            "tools": tools,
   315	            "tool_choice": tool_choice,
   316	            "response_format": response_format,
   317	            "reasoning_budget_tokens": reasoning_budget_tokens,
   318	            "cache_prompt": cache_prompt,
   319	        })
   320	
   321	        if self._index < len(self.responses):
   322	            resp = self.responses[self._index]
   323	            self._index += 1
   324	            return resp
   325	
   326	        # Default fallback response
   327	        return ProviderResponse(
   328	            content="Mock LLM response: Analysis complete.",
   329	            model="mock-provider",
   330	            duration_seconds=0.01,
   331	            finish_reason="stop",
   332	        )

--- [Step 65 | THINKING] ---
I'm noting that malformed JSON args get wrapped and passed to validation, which usually fails as INPUT_VALIDATION_FAILED unless the tool's fields are all optional, in which case the garbage input might slip through unnoticed. This seems like an edge case worth flagging around Pydantic's default handling of extra fields.

Looking at tool_call id generation, I see a real collision risk: IDs are built from a per-turn index plus a second-resolution timestamp, so two turns within the same second could produce identical ids, making tool_call_id non-unique and provenance ambiguous for ledger entries and artifacts. There's also no enforcement that model-supplied ids are unique, so a malicious or buggy model could reuse an id and corrupt evidence tracing. Separately, there's no retry logic anywhere, which avoids duplicate execution but means any transient llama-server failure just fails the whole run, and there's no handling for `n`>1 responses or truncated "length" finish reasons beyond the normal validation rejection.

Now I'm noting that reasoning_content isn't passed along in assistant messages, which seems fine, but the reported "model identity" is actually just the configured profile id, never the model string the server actually returned—so the report can misleadingly present configuration as fact. Response content gets clipped to 2000 chars in the ledger, and there's no cap on how many tool calls a single turn can contain, so a model emitting hundreds of tool calls in one turn would trigger hundreds of executions unless stopped by max_turns—a potential DoS vector from a runaway or injected model. Next I want to look at the gateway and model manager code.



--- [Step 66 | TOOL INVOCATION: Bash] ---
{
  "command": "cat -n intelligence/gateway.py | sed -n 1,296p | grep -v '^\\s*[0-9]*\\s*$' | head -300",
  "description": "Read model gateway"
}

[TOOL RESULT | SUCCESS]
     1	"""
     2	KAVACH AI 2.0 - Model Gateway Layer.
     3	Enforces the architectural invariant:
     4	  CONTROLLER -> MODEL GATEWAY -> MODEL PROVIDER
     6	Normalizes request schemas, tool definitions, tool calls, and reasoning tokens
     7	across heterogeneous models (Qwen 4B, Qwen 9B, future Qwen 4, OpenAI-compatible).
     8	Provides capability negotiation so the controller never branches on model names.
     9	"""
    11	from __future__ import annotations
    12	import json
    13	import logging
    14	from typing import List, Dict, Any, Optional
    15	import httpx
    17	from intelligence.provider import ReasoningProvider, ProviderResponse, ToolCall
    18	from intelligence.capabilities import ModelProfile, ModelCapabilities
    20	logger = logging.getLogger("kavach.intelligence.gateway")
    23	class ModelGateway:
    24	    """
    25	    Gateway between the Autonomous Investigation Controller and backend model providers.
    26	    Normalizes inputs and outputs, negotiates capabilities, and inspects server runtime properties.
    27	    """
    29	    def __init__(
    30	        self,
    31	        provider: ReasoningProvider,
    32	        profile: ModelProfile,
    33	    ) -> None:
    34	        # Fail-closed: the gateway never guesses which model it is talking to. Profile
    35	        # resolution (ModelProfileRegistry / ModelManager.create_gateway) happens outside.
    36	        if not isinstance(profile, ModelProfile):
    37	            raise TypeError(
    38	                f"ModelGateway requires a resolved ModelProfile, got {type(profile).__name__}. "
    39	                "Resolve it via MODEL_PROFILES[model_id] or ModelManager.create_gateway()."
    40	            )
    41	        self.provider = provider
    42	        self.profile = profile
    43	        self._capabilities: ModelCapabilities = self.profile.capabilities.model_copy()
    45	    @property
    46	    def capabilities(self) -> ModelCapabilities:
    47	        """Return the effective capabilities of the model."""
    48	        return self._capabilities
    50	    async def discover_runtime_capabilities(self) -> ModelCapabilities:
    51	        """
    52	        Probe runtime server endpoint (e.g. /props or /v1/models on llama-server)
    53	        to discover actual context length, model name, and parameter limits.
    54	        Updates self._capabilities with verified evidence.
    55	        """
    56	        base_url = getattr(self.provider, "base_url", None)
    57	        if not base_url:
    58	            return self._capabilities
    60	        # Attempt to probe llama-server /props endpoint
    61	        props_url = base_url.replace("/v1", "/props")
    62	        try:
    63	            async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=2.0) as client:
    64	                res = await client.get(props_url)
    65	                if res.status_code == 200:
    66	                    data = res.json()
    67	                    default_generation = data.get("default_generation_settings", {})
    68	                    n_ctx = default_generation.get("n_ctx") or data.get("n_ctx")
    69	                    if n_ctx and isinstance(n_ctx, int):
    70	                        self._capabilities.max_context = n_ctx
    71	                        logger.info(f"Discovered runtime context window: {n_ctx} from /props")
    72	        except Exception:
    73	            pass
    75	        return self._capabilities
    77	    def normalize_tool_definitions(
    78	        self,
    79	        tools: Optional[List[Dict[str, Any]]],
    80	    ) -> Optional[List[Dict[str, Any]]]:
    81	        """
    82	        Normalize tool schemas according to the model's capabilities.
    83	        If the model does not support parallel tool calls or native schemas,
    84	        this sanitizes parameter declarations.
    85	        """
    86	        if not tools:
    87	            return None
    89	        normalized = []
    90	        for t in tools:
    91	            # Ensure OpenAI-compatible function wrapping
    92	            if "type" in t and "function" in t:
    93	                normalized.append(t)
    94	            elif "name" in t:
    95	                # Wrap unwrapped schema
    96	                normalized.append({
    97	                    "type": "function",
    98	                    "function": {
    99	                        "name": t["name"],
   100	                        "description": t.get("description", ""),
   101	                        "parameters": t.get("parameters", {"type": "object", "properties": {}}),
   102	                    }
   103	                })
   104	            else:
   105	                normalized.append(t)
   107	        return normalized
   109	    @staticmethod
   110	    def estimate_prompt_tokens(messages: List[Dict[str, Any]], tools: Optional[List[Dict[str, Any]]] = None) -> int:
   111	        """Heuristic token estimation for messages and tool schemas."""
   112	        total_chars = 0
   113	        for m in messages:
   114	            content = m.get("content")
   115	            if isinstance(content, str):
   116	                total_chars += len(content)
   117	            elif isinstance(content, list):
   118	                total_chars += sum(len(str(part)) for part in content)
   119	            tool_calls = m.get("tool_calls")
   120	            if tool_calls and isinstance(tool_calls, list):
   121	                total_chars += sum(len(str(tc)) for tc in tool_calls)
   122	        if tools:
   123	            total_chars += len(json.dumps(tools))
   124	        # 1 token ~= 3.5 - 4 chars plus message boundary framing
   125	        return max(1, (total_chars // 4) + len(messages) * 4 + 16)
   127	    def calculate_context_budget(
   128	        self,
   129	        messages: List[Dict[str, Any]],
   130	        tools: Optional[List[Dict[str, Any]]] = None,
   131	        requested_max_tokens: int = 2048,
   132	        requested_reasoning_budget: Optional[int] = None,
   133	        safety_margin: int = 128,
   134	        input_tokens_override: Optional[int] = None,
   135	    ) -> Dict[str, int]:
   136	        """
   137	        Enforce strict invariant:
   138	        input tokens + reasoning budget + output budget + safety margin <= effective context.
   139	        """
   140	        effective_context = (
   141	            min(self.profile.context_window, self._capabilities.max_context)
   142	        )
   143	        input_tokens = (
   144	            input_tokens_override
   145	            if input_tokens_override is not None
   146	            else self.estimate_prompt_tokens(messages, tools)
   147	        )
   149	        available_for_generation = max(0, effective_context - input_tokens - safety_margin)
   150	        # ponytail: temporary guard only. Clamping max_tokens does not make an oversized prompt fit;
   151	        # the request will still overflow the context. Replace with ContextAssembler (select relevant
   152	        # evidence/hypothesis/artifact state into an exact budget) instead of truncating the output.
   153	        if available_for_generation <= 0:
   154	            logger.warning(
   155	                f"Prompt tokens ({input_tokens}) + safety margin ({safety_margin}) exceed effective context ({effective_context}). Clamping to minimal output."
   156	            )
   157	            return {
   158	                "input_tokens": input_tokens,
   159	                "reasoning_budget_tokens": 0,
   160	                "max_tokens": min(32, effective_context),
   161	                "effective_context": effective_context,
   162	                "safety_margin": safety_margin,
   163	            }
   165	        # Apply capability constraints
   166	        effective_reasoning_budget = 0
   167	        if self._capabilities.reasoning and self._capabilities.supports_budget_tokens:
   168	            if requested_reasoning_budget is not None and requested_reasoning_budget > 0:
   169	                # Reserve at least a minimal output slice for final text or tool calling
   170	                min_output_reserve = min(128, max(32, available_for_generation // 4))
   171	                max_reasoning_possible = max(0, available_for_generation - min_output_reserve)
   172	                effective_reasoning_budget = min(requested_reasoning_budget, max_reasoning_possible)
   173	            else:
   174	                effective_reasoning_budget = requested_reasoning_budget or 0
   175	        elif not self._capabilities.reasoning:
   176	            effective_reasoning_budget = 0
   178	        # Remaining tokens allocated to max_tokens output budget
   179	        max_output_possible = max(16, available_for_generation - (effective_reasoning_budget or 0))
   180	        safe_max_tokens = min(requested_max_tokens, max_output_possible)
   182	        return {
   183	            "input_tokens": input_tokens,
   184	            "reasoning_budget_tokens": effective_reasoning_budget,
   185	            "max_tokens": safe_max_tokens,
   186	            "effective_context": effective_context,
   187	            "safety_margin": safety_margin,
   188	        }
   190	    def normalize_request(
   191	        self,
   192	        messages: List[Dict[str, Any]],
   193	        tools: Optional[List[Dict[str, Any]]] = None,
   194	        tool_choice: Optional[str | Dict[str, Any]] = None,
   195	        max_tokens: int = 2048,
   196	        reasoning_budget_tokens: Optional[int] = None,
   197	    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   198	        """
   199	        Normalize request payload across providers.
   200	        Ensures input tokens + reasoning budget + output budget + safety margin <= effective context.
   201	        """
   202	        norm_tools = self.normalize_tool_definitions(tools)
   204	        budget = self.calculate_context_budget(
   205	            messages=messages,
   206	            tools=norm_tools,
   207	            requested_max_tokens=max_tokens,
   208	            requested_reasoning_budget=reasoning_budget_tokens,
   209	        )
   211	        effective_budget = budget["reasoning_budget_tokens"]
   212	        if not self._capabilities.supports_budget_tokens:
   213	            effective_budget = None
   215	        return {
   216	            "messages": messages,
   217	            "tools": norm_tools,
   218	            "tool_choice": tool_choice or "auto",
   219	            "max_tokens": budget["max_tokens"],
   220	            "reasoning_budget_tokens": effective_budget,
   221	            "context_budget": budget,
   222	        }
   224	    def normalize_response(self, response: ProviderResponse) -> ProviderResponse:
   225	        """
   226	        Ensure response strictly adheres to canonical KAVACH ProviderResponse:
   227	        - tool_calls have canonical id, name, and parsed dictionary arguments
   228	        - reasoning_content is preserved
   229	        - finish_reason is standardized
   230	        """
   231	        normalized_calls: List[ToolCall] = []
   232	        for call in response.tool_calls:
   233	            # If arguments were returned as a raw unparsed string, attempt parse
   234	            args = call.arguments
   235	            if isinstance(args, dict) and "raw" in args and len(args) == 1:
   236	                raw_str = args["raw"]
   237	                if isinstance(raw_str, str):
   238	                    try:
   239	                        parsed = json.loads(raw_str)
   240	                        if isinstance(parsed, dict):
   241	                            args = parsed
   242	                    except Exception:
   243	                        pass
   245	            normalized_calls.append(
   246	                ToolCall(
   247	                    id=call.id,
   248	                    name=call.name,
   249	                    arguments=args if isinstance(args, dict) else {"raw": str(args)},
   250	                )
   251	            )
   253	        response.tool_calls = normalized_calls
   254	        return response
   256	    async def chat_complete(
   257	        self,
   258	        messages: List[Dict[str, Any]],
   259	        tools: Optional[List[Dict[str, Any]]] = None,
   260	        tool_choice: Optional[str | Dict[str, Any]] = None,
   261	        response_format: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
   262	        temperature: float = 0.1,
   263	        max_tokens: int = 2048,
   264	        reasoning_budget_tokens: Optional[int] = None,
   265	        cache_prompt: bool = True,
   266	    ) -> ProviderResponse:
   267	        """
   268	        Execute chat completion through gateway normalization pipeline:
   269	        1. Normalize request
   270	        2. Execute via underlying provider
   271	        3. Normalize response
   272	        """
   273	        norm = self.normalize_request(
   274	            messages=messages,
   275	            tools=tools,
   276	            tool_choice=tool_choice,
   277	            max_tokens=max_tokens,
   278	            reasoning_budget_tokens=reasoning_budget_tokens,
   279	        )
   281	        raw_resp = await self.provider.chat_complete(
   282	            messages=norm["messages"],
   283	            tools=norm["tools"],
   284	            tool_choice=norm["tool_choice"],
   285	            response_format=response_format,
   286	            temperature=temperature,
   287	            max_tokens=norm["max_tokens"],
   288	            reasoning_budget_tokens=norm["reasoning_budget_tokens"],
   289	            cache_prompt=cache_prompt,
   290	        )
   292	        return self.normalize_response(raw_resp)
   294	    async def is_available(self) -> bool:
   295	        """Check availability of underlying provider."""
   296	        return await self.provider.is_available()

    74	        if model_id not in MODEL_PROFILES:
    75	            return {"supported": False, "reason": f"Unknown model profile '{model_id}'"}
    76

    84	
    85	        warnings = []
    86	        if total_ram_gb < min_ram:
    87	            return {
    88	                "supported": False,
    89	                "reason": f"Insufficient host RAM ({total_ram_gb}GB total < {min_ram}GB minimum required)",
    90	                "total_ram_gb": total_ram_gb,

    92	            }
    93	        elif total_ram_gb < rec_ram:
    94	            warnings.append(
    95	                f"Host RAM ({total_ram_gb}GB) meets minimum ({min_ram}GB) but is below recommended ({rec_ram}GB). Performance may degrade."
    96	            )
    97	
    98	        return {
    99	            "supported": True,
   100	            "model_id": model_id,
   101	            "total_ram_gb": total_ram_gb,
   102	            "available_ram_gb": available_ram_gb,

   208	def get_inference_profile(name: InferenceProfileName | str) -> InferenceProfile:

   213	        except ValueError:

--- [Step 71 | THINKING] ---
I'm realizing the standard profile only gives 256 output tokens with a 128 reasoning budget inside a 4096-token context window for this small model — that's extremely tight for an autonomous forensic investigator role. After accounting for output and reasoning tokens, there's really only about 3584 tokens left for the system prompt, initial observations, and tool schemas combined.

I also notice `get_inference_profile` silently falls back to STANDARD on an unknown profile name, which undercuts the fail-closed design claims elsewhere. Next I want to look at domain/evidence.py, the risk policy engine, and the console HTML, then the manifest triage analyzer, test files, frontend build, and finally the DB integration through docker.

    10	from datetime import datetime, timezone
    11	from enum import Enum
    12	from typing import Optional, Dict, Any, List
    13	from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
    14	
    15	
    16	class EvidenceOrigin(str, Enum):
    17	    STATIC = "STATIC"
    18	    DYNAMIC = "DYNAMIC"
    19	    NETWORK = "NETWORK"
    20	    ML_INFERENCE = "ML_INFERENCE"
    21	    AI_INTERPRETATION = "AI_INTERPRETATION"
    22	    RECONSTRUCTED_VERIFIED = "RECONSTRUCTED_VERIFIED"
    23	
    24	
    25	class EvidenceSeverity(str, Enum):
    26	    CRITICAL = "CRITICAL"
    27	    HIGH = "HIGH"
    28	    MEDIUM = "MEDIUM"
    29	    LOW = "LOW"
    30	    INFO = "INFO"
    31	
    32	
    33	class EvidenceConfidence(str, Enum):
    34	    HIGH = "HIGH"
    35	    MEDIUM = "MEDIUM"
    36	    LOW = "LOW"
    37	
    38	
    39	class EvidenceLocation(BaseModel):
    40	    file_path: Optional[str] = None
    41	    class_name: Optional[str] = None
    42	    method_name: Optional[str] = None
    43	    line_number: Optional[int] = None
    44	    offset: Optional[int] = None
    45	    manifest_element: Optional[str] = None
    46	
    47	
    48	class Evidence(BaseModel):
    49	    id: str = Field(default_factory=lambda: str(uuid.uuid4()))
    50	    run_id: str = ""
    51	    scan_id: str = ""  # Backward compatibility alias
    52	    origin: EvidenceOrigin
    53	    stage: str
    54	    analyzer: str
    55	    analyzer_version: str = "1.0.0"
    56	    category: str
    57	    type: str
    58	    title: str
    59	    description: Optional[str] = None
    60	    severity: EvidenceSeverity = EvidenceSeverity.MEDIUM
    61	    confidence: EvidenceConfidence = EvidenceConfidence.HIGH
    62	    source: str
    63	    artifact: Optional[str] = None
    64	    location: Optional[EvidenceLocation] = None
    65	    timestamp: datetime = Field(default_factory=lambda: datetime.now(timezone.utc))
    66	    raw_reference: Optional[Any] = None
    67	    normalized_value: Optional[Any] = None
    68	    metadata: Dict[str, Any] = Field(default_factory=dict)
    69	    # Provenance for agent-produced evidence (set by the investigation controller).
    70	    source_artifact_id: Optional[str] = None
    71	    source_execution_id: Optional[str] = None
    72	    source_tool_call_id: Optional[str] = None
    73	
    74	    def model_post_init(self, __context: Any) -> None:
    75	        if not self.run_id and self.scan_id:
    76	            self.run_id = self.scan_id
    77	        elif not self.scan_id and self.run_id:
    78	            self.scan_id = self.run_id
    79	
    80	
    81	# Canonical alias for 2.0 vocabulary
    82	EvidenceItem = Evidence
    83	
    84	
    85	class EvidenceStore(BaseModel):
    86	    run_id: str = ""
    87	    scan_id: str = ""
    88	    items: List[Evidence] = Field(default_factory=list)
    89	
    90	    def model_post_init(self, __context: Any) -> None:
    91	        if not self.run_id and self.scan_id:
    92	            self.run_id = self.scan_id
    93	        elif not self.scan_id and self.run_id:
    94	            self.scan_id = self.run_id
    95	
    96	    def add(self, item: Evidence) -> None:
    97	        self.items.append(item)
    98	
    99	    def filter_by_origin(self, origin: EvidenceOrigin) -> List[Evidence]:
   100	        return [item for item in self.items if item.origin == origin]
   101	
   102	    def filter_by_category(self, category: str) -> List[Evidence]:
   103	        return [item for item in self.items if item.category == category]
     1	"""
     2	Deterministic Risk Policy Engine for KAVACH AI 2.0.
     3	Calculates transparent, reproducible risk assessments from canonical Evidence.
     4	Operates completely offline without LLM dependencies.
     5	"""
     6	
     7	from __future__ import annotations
     8	import os
     9	import yaml
    10	from pathlib import Path
    11	from typing import List, Dict, Any, Optional
    12	
    13	from domain.evidence import Evidence, EvidenceSeverity
    14	from domain.risk import RiskAssessment, ThreatLevel, RiskContributor
    15	
    16	
    17	SEVERITY_SCORES = {
    18	    EvidenceSeverity.CRITICAL: 100.0,
    19	    EvidenceSeverity.HIGH: 75.0,
    20	    EvidenceSeverity.MEDIUM: 40.0,
    21	    EvidenceSeverity.LOW: 15.0,
    22	    EvidenceSeverity.INFO: 0.0,
    23	}
    24	
    25	
    26	DEFAULT_POLICY: Dict[str, Any] = {
    27	    "policy_name": "v2_banking",
    28	    "policy_version": "2.0.0",
    29	    "weights": {
    30	        "signatures": 0.30,
    31	        "evasion": 0.25,
    32	        "permissions": 0.20,
    33	        "behavior": 0.15,
    34	        "crypto": 0.10,
    35	    },
    36	    "thresholds": {
    37	        "critical": 85,
    38	        "high": 70,
    39	        "medium": 40,
    40	        "low": 20,
    41	    },
    42	    "escalation_rules": [
    43	        {
    44	            "id": "RULE_ACCESSIBILITY_OVERLAY_COMBO",
    45	            "title": "Accessibility Service & Overlay Combination",
    46	            "description": "App requests both Accessibility Service and System Alert Window, the canonical Android banking overlay fraud pattern.",
    47	            "required_evidence_types": [
    48	                "android.permission.BIND_ACCESSIBILITY_SERVICE",
    49	                "android.permission.SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW",
    50	            ],
    51	            "min_score": 90,
    52	            "threat_level": "CRITICAL",
    53	        },
    54	        {
    55	            "id": "RULE_KNOWN_BANKING_TROJAN_SIGNATURE",
    56	            "title": "Known Banking Trojan Family Match",
    57	            "description": "YARA rule matched a known high-severity banking trojan family signature.",
    58	            "signature_patterns": [
    59	                "cerberus", "hydra", "anatsa", "teabot", "hook", "ermac", "sharkbot", "alien", "bankbot"
    60	            ],
    61	            "min_score": 95,
    62	            "threat_level": "CRITICAL",
    63	        },
    64	        {
    65	            "id": "RULE_DEX_EVASION_PACKER_COMBO",
    66	            "title": "Obfuscator/Packer with Dynamic DEX Execution",
    67	            "description": "Sample utilizes a third-party commercial packer/protector combined with dynamic DEX class loading.",
    68	            "required_categories": ["packer", "evasion"],
    69	            "min_score": 82,
    70	            "threat_level": "HIGH",
    71	        },
    72	        {
    73	            "id": "RULE_SMS_STEALER_COMBO",
    74	            "title": "SMS Interception & Network Exfiltration Capability",
    75	            "description": "App requests SMS interception/reading permissions together with internet access.",
    76	            "required_evidence_types": [
    77	                "android.permission.RECEIVE_SMS",
    78	                "android.permission.INTERNET",
    79	            ],
    80	            "min_score": 75,
    81	            "threat_level": "HIGH",
    82	        },
    83	        {
    84	            "id": "RULE_ZIP_EVASION_ANOMALY",
    85	            "title": "Anti-Analysis Archive Manipulation",
    86	            "description": "Corrupted ZIP headers or non-standard compression methods designed to defeat static inspection.",
    87	            "required_evidence_types": [
    88	                "CORRUPTED_ZIP_ENTRY",
    89	                "MANIFEST_COMPRESSION_EVASION",
    90	                "ANDROGUARD_ZIP_EVASION",
    91	            ],
    92	            "min_score": 72,
    93	            "threat_level": "HIGH",
    94	        },
    95	    ],
    96	}
    97	
    98	
    99	class PolicyEngine:
   100	    def __init__(self, policy_path: Optional[str] = None):
   101	        self.policy: Dict[str, Any] = DEFAULT_POLICY
   102	        if policy_path and os.path.exists(policy_path):
   103	            try:
   104	                with open(policy_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   105	                    loaded = yaml.safe_load(f)
   106	                    if isinstance(loaded, dict):
   107	                        self.policy = loaded
   108	            except Exception:
   109	                pass
   110	        else:
   111	            default_yaml_path = os.path.join(
   112	                os.path.dirname(__file__), "policies", "v2_banking.yaml"
   113	            )
   114	            if os.path.exists(default_yaml_path):
   115	                try:
   116	                    with open(default_yaml_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   117	                        loaded = yaml.safe_load(f)
   118	                        if isinstance(loaded, dict):
   119	                            self.policy = loaded
   120	                except Exception:
   121	                    pass
   122	
   123	    def evaluate(self, run_id: str, evidence_list: List[Evidence]) -> RiskAssessment:
   124	        weights: Dict[str, float] = self.policy.get("weights", DEFAULT_POLICY["weights"])
   125	        thresholds: Dict[str, int] = self.policy.get("thresholds", DEFAULT_POLICY["thresholds"])
   126	        escalation_rules: List[Dict[str, Any]] = self.policy.get("escalation_rules", [])
   127	
   128	        # Group evidence by category
   129	        category_evidence: Dict[str, List[Evidence]] = {
   130	            "signatures": [],
   131	            "evasion": [],
   132	            "permissions": [],
   133	            "behavior": [],
   134	            "crypto": [],
   135	        }
   136	
   137	        all_types: set[str] = set()
   138	        all_categories: set[str] = set()
   139	
   140	        for ev in evidence_list:
   141	            all_types.add(ev.type)
   142	            all_categories.add(ev.category)
   143	
   144	            # Map domain evidence categories to scoring categories
   145	            cat = ev.category.lower()
   146	            if cat in ("signature", "signatures", "yara"):
   147	                category_evidence["signatures"].append(ev)
   148	            elif cat in ("evasion", "packer", "obfuscator", "protector"):
   149	                category_evidence["evasion"].append(ev)
   150	            elif cat in ("permission", "permissions"):
   151	                category_evidence["permissions"].append(ev)
   152	            elif cat in ("behavior", "behavioral", "quark"):
   153	                category_evidence["behavior"].append(ev)
   154	            elif cat in ("crypto", "cryptography"):
   155	                category_evidence["crypto"].append(ev)
   156	            else:
   157	                # Default to behavior
   158	                category_evidence["behavior"].append(ev)
   159	
   160	        # Calculate sub-scores per category (0 to 100)
   161	        sub_scores: Dict[str, float] = {}
   162	        for cat, items in category_evidence.items():
   163	            if not items:
   164	                sub_scores[cat] = 0.0
   165	                continue
   166	            # Non-linear severity saturation: max_score * 0.7 + average_score * 0.3
   167	            scores = [SEVERITY_SCORES.get(item.severity, 0.0) for item in items]
   168	            max_s = max(scores)
   169	            avg_s = sum(scores) / len(scores)
   170	            sub_scores[cat] = round(min(100.0, (max_s * 0.7) + (avg_s * 0.3)), 1)
   171	
   172	        # Compute initial weighted composite score
   173	        composite_score: float = sum(
   174	            sub_scores.get(cat, 0.0) * weights.get(cat, 0.0) for cat in weights
   175	        )
   176	
   177	        top_contributors: List[RiskContributor] = []
   178	
   179	        # Check hard escalation rules
   180	        escalated_threat_level: Optional[ThreatLevel] = None
   181	
   182	        for rule in escalation_rules:
   183	            rule_id = rule.get("id", "")
   184	            rule_title = rule.get("title", rule_id)
   185	            min_score = float(rule.get("min_score", 0))
   186	            rule_threat = ThreatLevel(rule.get("threat_level", "HIGH"))
   187	
   188	            triggered = False
   189	            matched_evidence_ids: List[str] = []
   190	
   191	            # 1. Required types check
   192	            req_types = rule.get("required_evidence_types")
   193	            if req_types:
   194	                # If rule specifies a list where all or any match
   195	                if rule_id == "RULE_ZIP_EVASION_ANOMALY":
   196	                    # Any match is sufficient
   197	                    for ev in evidence_list:
   198	                        if ev.type in req_types:
   199	                            triggered = True
   200	                            matched_evidence_ids.append(ev.id)
   201	                else:
   202	                    # All must be present
   203	                    if all(t in all_types for t in req_types):
   204	                        triggered = True
   205	                        matched_evidence_ids = [
   206	                            ev.id for ev in evidence_list if ev.type in req_types
   207	                        ]
   208	
   209	            # 2. Signature patterns check
   210	            sig_patterns = rule.get("signature_patterns")
   211	            if sig_patterns:
   212	                for ev in category_evidence["signatures"]:
   213	                    val_lower = (ev.normalized_value or ev.title).lower()
   214	                    if any(p in val_lower for p in sig_patterns):
   215	                        triggered = True
   216	                        matched_evidence_ids.append(ev.id)
   217	
   218	            # 3. Required categories check
   219	            req_cats = rule.get("required_categories")
   220	            if req_cats:
   221	                if all(c in all_categories for c in req_cats):
   222	                    triggered = True
   223	                    matched_evidence_ids = [
   224	                        ev.id for ev in evidence_list if ev.category in req_cats
   225	                    ]
   226	
   227	            if triggered:
   228	                composite_score = max(composite_score, min_score)
   229	                if escalated_threat_level is None or rule_threat == ThreatLevel.CRITICAL:
   230	                    escalated_threat_level = rule_threat
   231	
   232	                top_contributors.append(
   233	                    RiskContributor(
   234	                        label=rule_title,
   235	                        category="escalation_rule",
   236	                        weight=1.0,
   237	                        evidence_ids=matched_evidence_ids,
   238	                        explanation=rule.get("description", ""),
   239	                    )
   240	                )
   241	
   242	        # Floor based on raw maximum severity present
   243	        if any(ev.severity == EvidenceSeverity.CRITICAL for ev in evidence_list):
   244	            composite_score = max(composite_score, 80.0)
   245	            if escalated_threat_level is None:
   246	                escalated_threat_level = ThreatLevel.HIGH
   247	        elif any(ev.severity == EvidenceSeverity.HIGH for ev in evidence_list):
   248	            composite_score = max(composite_score, 60.0)
   249	
   250	        # Add top individual evidence items as contributors if not already saturated
   251	        high_severity_items = [
   252	            ev for ev in evidence_list if ev.severity in (EvidenceSeverity.CRITICAL, EvidenceSeverity.HIGH)
   253	        ]
   254	        for item in high_severity_items[:5]:
   255	            top_contributors.append(
   256	                RiskContributor(
   257	                    label=item.title,
   258	                    category=item.category,
   259	                    weight=0.5,
   260	                    evidence_ids=[item.id],
   261	                    explanation=item.description,
   262	                )
   263	            )
   264	
   265	        final_score = int(round(min(100.0, max(0.0, composite_score))))
   266	
   267	        # Determine threat level
   268	        if escalated_threat_level:
   269	            threat_level = escalated_threat_level
   270	        elif final_score >= thresholds.get("critical", 85):
   271	            threat_level = ThreatLevel.CRITICAL
   272	        elif final_score >= thresholds.get("high", 70):
   273	            threat_level = ThreatLevel.HIGH
   274	        elif final_score >= thresholds.get("medium", 40):
   275	            threat_level = ThreatLevel.MEDIUM
   276	        elif final_score >= thresholds.get("low", 20):
   277	            threat_level = ThreatLevel.LOW
   278	        else:
   279	            threat_level = ThreatLevel.SAFE
   280	
   281	        # Summary verdict text
   282	        if threat_level == ThreatLevel.CRITICAL:
   283	            summary_verdict = f"MALICIOUS: High-risk Android malware or banking trojan detected (Score: {final_score}/100)."
   284	        elif threat_level == ThreatLevel.HIGH:
   285	            summary_verdict = f"SUSPICIOUS / HIGH RISK: Significant evasion techniques or suspicious permissions detected (Score: {final_score}/100)."
   286	        elif threat_level == ThreatLevel.MEDIUM:
   287	            summary_verdict = f"POTENTIALLY UNWANTED / MEDIUM RISK: Elevated permissions or weak cryptographic configurations (Score: {final_score}/100)."
   288	        elif threat_level == ThreatLevel.LOW:
   289	            summary_verdict = f"LOW RISK: Minor findings with low severity indicators (Score: {final_score}/100)."
   290	        else:
   291	            summary_verdict = f"SAFE / BENIGN: No malicious indicators detected (Score: {final_score}/100)."
   292	
   293	        return RiskAssessment(
   294	            run_id=run_id,
   295	            composite_score=final_score,
   296	            threat_level=threat_level,
   297	            policy_name=self.policy.get("policy_name", "v2_banking"),
   298	            policy_version=self.policy.get("policy_version", "2.0.0"),
   299	            sub_scores=sub_scores,
   300	            score_weights=weights,
   301	            top_contributors=top_contributors,
   302	            summary_verdict=summary_verdict,
   303	            metadata={"evidence_count": len(evidence_list)},
   304	        )

--- [Step 73 | THINKING] ---
I'm noting that any single HIGH severity evidence pushes the score floor to 60, crossing the medium threshold of 40, so a fixture with something like CONTAINER_EVASION_DETECTED would land as "POTENTIALLY UNWANTED / MEDIUM RISK" with category defaulting to "behavior."

I also notice the SMS stealer combo rule depends on exact manifest evidence naming, the critical verdict text makes a strong malware claim, and evidence weight saturation caps out fine at scale. More concerning is that the PolicyEngine silently swallows YAML load errors and falls back to the default policy without any signal, and since risk is recalculated fresh on every report request rather than persisted, a policy file change could silently alter historical reports with no policy hash recorded in the ledger for reproducibility.

            canonical_path=selected_bin,
            sha256="",
            is_kavach_managed=selected_bin.startswith("/opt/kavach"),
            is_setuid=False,
            cap_bwrap=False,
            cap_userns=False,
            cap_pidns=False,
            cap_netns=False,
            cap_openat2=False,
            cap_seccomp=False,
            functional_probe_passed=False,
            openat2_supported=False,
            userns_supported=False,
            netns_supported=False,
            rejection_reason=f"Failed to query bubblewrap version: {e}",
        )

    # Verify version floor >= 0.12.0
    def _parse_version(v: str) -> tuple[int, ...]:
        try:
            return tuple(int(x) for x in v.split(".") if x.isdigit())
        except Exception:
            return (0, 0, 0)

    ver_tuple = _parse_version(version_str)
    cap_bwrap = ver_tuple >= (0, 12, 0)
    if not cap_bwrap:
        return BwrapCapability(
            available=False,
            version=version_str,
            canonical_path=selected_bin,
            sha256="",
            is_kavach_managed=selected_bin.startswith("/opt/kavach"),
            is_setuid=False,
            cap_bwrap=False,
            cap_userns=False,
            cap_pidns=False,
            cap_netns=False,
            cap_openat2=False,
            cap_seccomp=False,
            functional_probe_passed=False,
            openat2_supported=False,
            userns_supported=False,
            netns_supported=False,
            rejection_reason=(
                f"Bubblewrap version '{version_str}' is below minimum supported baseline 0.12.0 "
                "(vulnerable to CVE-2026-87766 symlink traversal during sandbox setup)."
            ),
        )

    # Compute binary SHA-256
    with open(selected_bin, "rb") as f:
        sha256 = hashlib.sha256(f.read()).hexdigest()

    base_mounts = [
        "--ro-bind", "/usr", "/usr",
        "--ro-bind", "/lib", "/lib",
        "--ro-bind", "/bin", "/bin",
    ]
    if os.path.exists("/lib64"):
        base_mounts.extend(["--ro-bind", "/lib64", "/lib64"])

    # Discrete capability probes
    # A. User Namespace probe
    cap_userns = False
    try:
        r = subprocess.run([selected_bin] + base_mounts + ["--unshare-user", "--", "/bin/true"], capture_output=True, timeout=3.0)
        cap_userns = (r.returncode == 0)
    except Exception:
        cap_userns = False

    # B. PID Namespace probe
    cap_pidns = False
    try:
        r = subprocess.run([selected_bin] + base_mounts + ["--proc", "/proc", "--unshare-pid", "--", "/bin/true"], capture_output=True, timeout=3.0)
        cap_pidns = (r.returncode == 0)
    except Exception:
        cap_pidns = False

    # C. Network Namespace probe
    cap_netns = False
    try:
        r = subprocess.run([selected_bin] + base_mounts + ["--unshare-net", "--", "/bin/true"], capture_output=True, timeout=3.0)
        cap_netns = (r.returncode == 0)
    except Exception:
        cap_netns = False

    # D. openat2 probe (Linux kernel >= 5.6)
    cap_openat2 = False
    try:
        release = os.uname().release.split("-")[0]
        kparts = tuple(int(x) for x in release.split(".")[:2] if x.isdigit())
        cap_openat2 = (kparts >= (5, 6))
    except Exception:
        cap_openat2 = True

    # E. seccomp probe
    cap_seccomp = os.path.exists("/proc/sys/kernel/seccomp")

    # F. Functional combined probe
    probe_cmd = [
        selected_bin,
        "--ro-bind", "/usr", "/usr",
        "--ro-bind", "/lib", "/lib",
        "--ro-bind", "/bin", "/bin",
        "--proc", "/proc",
        "--dev", "/dev",
        "--unshare-all",
        "--unshare-net",
    ]
    if os.path.exists("/lib64"):
        probe_cmd.extend(["--ro-bind", "/lib64", "/lib64"])
    probe_cmd.extend(["--", "/bin/true"])

    functional_passed = False
    rejection_reason = None
    try:
        probe_res = subprocess.run(probe_cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=5.0)
        if probe_res.returncode == 0:
            functional_passed = True
        else:
            rejection_reason = f"Functional probe failed (exit code {probe_res.returncode}): {probe_res.stderr.strip()}"
    except Exception as e:
        rejection_reason = f"Functional probe threw exception: {e}"

    is_avail = bool(cap_bwrap and cap_userns and cap_pidns and cap_netns and functional_passed)

    return BwrapCapability(
        available=is_avail,
        version=version_str,
        canonical_path=selected_bin,
        sha256=sha256,
        is_kavach_managed=selected_bin.startswith("/opt/kavach"),
        is_setuid=False,
        cap_bwrap=cap_bwrap,
        cap_userns=cap_userns,
        cap_pidns=cap_pidns,
        cap_netns=cap_netns,
        cap_openat2=cap_openat2,
        cap_seccomp=cap_seccomp,
        functional_probe_passed=functional_passed,
        openat2_supported=cap_openat2,
        userns_supported=cap_userns,
        netns_supported=cap_netns,
        rejection_reason=rejection_reason,
    )

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Move to the project root directory regardless of invocation path
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
set -e

# The 1.x frontend is unsupported; install its packages only on request.
WITH_FRONTEND=0
for arg in "$@"; do
    case "$arg" in
        --with-frontend) WITH_FRONTEND=1 ;;
        *) echo "Unknown option: $arg (supported: --with-frontend)"; exit 2 ;;
    esac
done

# ANSI styling
RED='\033[0;31m'
GREEN='\033[0;32m'
BLUE='\033[0;34m'
YELLOW='\033[1;33m'
NC='\033[0m' # No Color

echo -e "${BLUE}============================================================${NC}"
echo -e "${GREEN}🛡️  KAVACH AI — Initializing Setup Sequence...${NC}"
echo -e "${BLUE}============================================================${NC}"

# Check for Prerequisites
echo -e "\n${YELLOW}[Step 1/4] Checking System Prerequisites...${NC}"

# The backend targets Python 3.12 (CI and the appliance image use 3.12).
PYTHON_BIN="$(command -v python3.12 || command -v python3 || true)"
if [ -z "$PYTHON_BIN" ]; then
    echo -e "${RED}❌ Error: python3.12 (or python3) is not installed.${NC}"
    exit 1
fi
echo -e "  ✓ python found: $($PYTHON_BIN --version) ($PYTHON_BIN)"

if [ "$WITH_FRONTEND" -eq 1 ]; then
    if ! command -v node &> /dev/null; then
        echo -e "${RED}❌ Error: node (Node.js) is not installed.${NC}"
        exit 1
    fi
    echo -e "  ✓ node found: $(node --version)"

    if ! command -v npm &> /dev/null; then
        echo -e "${RED}❌ Error: npm is not installed.${NC}"
        exit 1
    fi
    echo -e "  ✓ npm found: $(npm --version)"
fi

if ! command -v java &> /dev/null; then
    echo -e "${YELLOW}⚠️  Warning: java (JRE 17) is missing. The JADX and APKTool forensic tools need it.${NC}"
else
    echo -e "  ✓ java found: $(java -version 2>&1 | head -n 1)"
fi


# Setup Virtual Environment and Backend Dependencies
echo -e "\n${YELLOW}[Step 2/4] Initializing Python Virtual Environment & Backend dependencies...${NC}"
cd backend

--- [Step 86 | THINKING] ---
I'm noticing CI disables a host security control system-wide via that sysctl setting, which raises the question of whether the user did the same on their dev machine. The bwrap binary in the kavach runtime lacks an AppArmor profile unlike the system one, so on Ubuntu 24.04 with restriction enabled, unprivileged userns creation would fail unless that sysctl was disabled—yet the probe passed functionally, which tells me something about this host's actual configuration.

I also notice other CI quirks: workflows trigger only on pull_request to main/master while ci-main runs on push, the Postgres image isn't pinned by digest, and pip is used despite setup-uv being installed, though GitHub Actions themselves are properly pinned by SHA. Next I want to verify the sysctl setting directly, check git history and remote diffs, then move to the frontend before spinning up Postgres via docker for integration tests and searching for a local GGUF model to run an end-to-end test with a mock model.

--- [Step 100 | THINKING] ---
Integration tests pass, so I want to quickly check what test_service_postgres and test_investigation_service actually verify before designing adversarial tests. I'm planning attacks like delimiter forgery through sanitize_model_text, and ledger exploits—truncation bypass, disabling the trigger as owner, rewriting and re-verifying the chain, appending after RUN_FINALIZED, and testing a lone surrogate payload that could crash the append logic.

I'm also mapping out more attack surfaces: forged cross-run parent lineage in ArtifactStore, undetected sha changes via direct UPDATE, staged artifacts without hash verification, fake evidence IDs accepted by the recorder, and a benign APK false-positive scenario involving classes.dex plus high-entropy assets. Other angles include ReDoS timing in search_strings, repair_container performance on large files, decompression bombs, prompt injection through manifest data, concurrency issues where two service instances interfere with each other's interrupted runs, duplicate tool_call ids, DEX artifact explosion, and misclassified timeouts. I confirmed all EventTypes are covered in the report mapping, so that's fine. Now I want to check service_testing.py for existing helpers before building these tests.

    28	def tool_turn(*calls: tuple, content: str = "") -> Dict[str, Any]:
    29	    """One scripted assistant turn that emits native tool calls: tool_turn(("name", {args}), ...)."""
    30	    return {"content": content, "tool_calls": [{"name": n, "arguments": a} for n, a in calls]}
    31	
    32	
    33	def text_turn(content: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    34	    return {"content": content, "tool_calls": []}
    35	
    36	
    37	class ScriptedLlamaServer:
    38	    """Replays scripted assistant turns in llama-server's response format and records every request."""
    39	
    40	    def __init__(self, turns: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> None:
    41	        self.turns = list(turns)
    42	        self.requests: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
    43	        self._call_counter = 0
    44	
    45	    def handler(self, request: httpx.Request) -> httpx.Response:
    46	        body = json.loads(request.content)
    47	        self.requests.append(body)
    48	        turn = self.turns.pop(0) if self.turns else text_turn("Investigation complete.")
    49	        tool_calls = []
    50	        for call in turn["tool_calls"]:
    51	            self._call_counter += 1
    52	            tool_calls.append({"id": f"call_{self._call_counter}", "type": "function",
    53	                               "function": {"name": call["name"], "arguments": json.dumps(call["arguments"])}})
    54	        message: Dict[str, Any] = {"role": "assistant", "content": turn["content"]}
    55	        if tool_calls:
    56	            message["tool_calls"] = tool_calls
    57	        return httpx.Response(200, json={
    58	            "model": LIVE_PROFILE,
    59	            "choices": [{"index": 0, "message": message, "finish_reason": "tool_calls" if tool_calls else "stop"}],
    60	            "usage": {"prompt_tokens": 100, "completion_tokens": 20},
    61	            "timings": {},
    62	        })
    63	
    64	    def provider(self) -> LlamaCppReasoningProvider:
    65	        return LlamaCppReasoningProvider(base_url="http://llama.test/v1", transport=httpx.MockTransport(self.handler))
    66	
    67	
    68	def controller_factory(server: ScriptedLlamaServer) -> Callable[[ArtifactStore, InvestigationLedger], InvestigationController]:
    69	    def factory(store: ArtifactStore, ledger: InvestigationLedger) -> InvestigationController:
    70	        gateway = ModelGateway(server.provider(), MODEL_PROFILES[LIVE_PROFILE])
    71	        return InvestigationController(gateway, build_demo_tool_registry(), artifact_store=store, ledger=ledger)
    72	    return factory

"""
InvestigationService and HTTP API: run lifecycle states, write ordering, the native tool protocol,
and the golden demo path from upload to verified report (in-memory stores; the PostgreSQL restart
proof lives in test_service_postgres.py).
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import asyncio
import json
import time
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Dict

import pytest
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient

from domain.execution_state import RUN_TERMINAL_STATES, ExecutionState, parse_run_state
from domain.investigation import InvestigationStatus, run_state_for
from ledger import EventType, InvestigationLedger
from service.api import create_app
from service.demo_fixture import PACKAGE_MARKER, build_demo_apk
from service.runs import MemoryRunRepository, RunNotRecorded, RunRecord
from service_testing import ScriptedLlamaServer, build_service, golden_script
from storage.store import ArtifactStore

TERMINAL = {"COMPLETED", "COMPLETED_NO_FINDINGS", "PARTIAL", "FAILED", "TIMEOUT", "INTERRUPTED"}


def _record(run_id: str = "run_a") -> RunRecord:
    from datetime import datetime, timezone
    return RunRecord(run_id=run_id, sample_id="sample_a", sample_sha256="0" * 64, file_name="a.apk",
                     size_bytes=1, created_at=datetime.now(timezone.utc))


def _wait_terminal(client: TestClient, run_id: str, timeout: float = 60.0) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        body = client.get(f"/investigations/{run_id}").json()
        if body["status"] in TERMINAL:
            return body
        time.sleep(0.05)
    raise AssertionError(f"run {run_id} did not finish: {body}")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- run lifecycle states


@pytest.mark.asyncio
@pytest.mark.parametrize("state", sorted(s.value for s in RUN_TERMINAL_STATES))
async def test_memory_repository_preserves_every_terminal_state(state: str):
    runs = MemoryRunRepository()
    created = await runs.create(_record())
    assert created.status == "QUEUED"
    await runs.mark_running("run_a")
    assert (await runs.get("run_a")).status == "RUNNING"
    await runs.mark_finished("run_a", state, "detail")
    stored = await runs.get("run_a")
    assert stored.status == state and stored.error == "detail"


@pytest.mark.asyncio
@pytest.mark.parametrize("bad", ["QUEUED", "RUNNING", "SKIPPED", "COMPLETED_WITH_LIMITATIONS", "NOPE"])
async def test_mark_finished_rejects_non_terminal_or_unknown_states(bad: str):
    runs = MemoryRunRepository()
    await runs.create(_record())
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        await runs.mark_finished("run_a", bad)
    assert (await runs.get("run_a")).status == "QUEUED"


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_lifecycle_update_of_unknown_run_raises():
    with pytest.raises(RunNotRecorded):
        await MemoryRunRepository().mark_finished("missing", "FAILED")


def test_run_state_set_is_exact():
    assert {s.value for s in RUN_TERMINAL_STATES} == TERMINAL
    assert parse_run_state("INTERRUPTED") == ExecutionState.INTERRUPTED

--- [Step 114 | THINKING] ---
I'm puzzled why verify_valid is coming back False despite the timestamp formatting looking consistent between stored and parsed values, so I'm trying to figure out if there's some sequence ordering issue with the events being iterated.

Found it: `_new_event` generates a fresh event_id, but my UPDATE only touches other columns while leaving the old event_id in place, so the hash gets computed against the new id while the stored row keeps the old one, causing a mismatch. I need to fix this by reconstructing the event body using the original event_id.

I'm also noticing something more important: even when verification is invalid, the report still renders a "SAFE" risk verdict — the report logic doesn't actually gate on ledger validity, which is itself a real finding. And separately, after erasing a run's ledger, a completed run gets misreported as "QUEUED" by the status logic, while an empty chain still verifies as valid — another gap worth flagging.

Checking PG2: since docker's default POSTGRES_USER is superuser, the app role in this audit container is almost certainly superuser too, and probably in the deployed appliance as well — I should check the compose file to confirm.

PG6 confirms a cross-instance issue: one instance marks a run INTERRUPTED while another instance keeps appending events afterward, including a conflicting second COMPLETED finalization, yet the API still reports INTERRUPTED and verification still passes — the ledger tolerates contradictory terminal states. Now let me fix the PG4 probe to preserve the event_id.

// ── Analysis ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

import { getMockAnalysisResult, getMockClustering, getMockMobSFReport } from './mock_data';

export async function getAnalysis(id: string): Promise<AnalysisResult> {
  try {
    return await request<AnalysisResult>(`/analysis/${id}`);
  } catch (err) {
    if (process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_ENABLE_DEMO_MOCKS === 'true') {
      return getMockAnalysisResult(id);
    }
    throw err;
  }
}

export async function getHistory(): Promise<HistoryItem[]> {
  return request<HistoryItem[]>('/history');
}

export async function triggerDynamic(id: string, uid?: string): Promise<{ status: string }> {
  return request(`/analysis/${id}/dynamic`, {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
    body: JSON.stringify({ uid }),
  });
}

export async function cancelAnalysis(id: string): Promise<{ status: string }> {
  return request(`/analysis/${id}/cancel`, { method: 'POST' });
}

export async function getReport(id: string): Promise<{ format: string; content: string }> {
  try {
    return await request(`/analysis/${id}/report`);
  } catch {
    return { format: 'json', content: JSON.stringify(getMockMobSFReport(id)) };
  }
}

export async function getClustering(id: string): Promise<{ graph: unknown; correlations: unknown }> {
  try {
    return await request(`/analysis/${id}/clustering`);
  } catch {
    return getMockClustering(id);
  }
}

// ── Chat ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

export async function chat(analysis_id: string, message: string, uid?: string): Promise<{ answer: string }> {
  return request('/chat', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
    body: JSON.stringify({ analysis_id, message, uid }),
  });
}

// ── SSE Stream (returns EventSource) ─────────────────────────────────────────

export function createAnalysisStream(id: string): EventSource {
  const token = typeof window !== 'undefined' ? localStorage.getItem('kavach_token') : '';
  return new EventSource(`${BASE}/analysis/${id}/stream?token=${token ?? ''}`);
}

// ── Sandbox Health ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

export async function getSandboxHealth(): Promise<Record<string, unknown>> {
  return request('/sandbox-health');
}

Based on the search results, here's what I found about JURIG:

## Overview

JURIG is an autonomous AI agent that decompiles Android apps, greps for secrets, hooks Frida, and captures live traffic through its own MITM proxy — all from a hacker-style terminal UI.

## Key Features

The tool is built with Go and includes:
- JURIG (Sundanese: *ghost*) — an autonomous AI agent that haunts your binaries
- Decompilation capabilities for Android applications
- Secret discovery functionality
- Frida integration for dynamic analysis
- Man-in-the-middle (MITM) proxy for traffic capture
- An interactive terminal user interface (TUI)

## Getting Started

Users can grab a build from the releases page and run commands like `jurig doctor` to check providers and toolchain, or `jurig` for first run which launches an interactive setup wizard. The tool auto-installs missing dependencies like jadx and apktool on demand.

## Source Code

The project is open-source and available at: https://github.com/ReverserID/JURIG

## Important Note

This tool is for authorized security research and education only, and users should analyze apps they own or have explicit permission to test.

## Overview

The agentic-malware-analysis project is a Kali-based Docker environment that pairs 50+ reverse engineering tools with MCP-connected disassembler backends (Binary Ninja or Ghidra) and a structured multi-phase orchestrator skill that turns a raw binary into a case directory of ranked evidence, validated hypotheses, component maps, and a prioritized deep-analysis plan with no human interaction required.

## Key Features

The project includes a Kali Linux container with 50+ RE and malware analysis tools, automatic MCP backend selection (Binary Ninja or Ghidra), a malware-analysis-orchestrator skill for Claude Code and Codex CLI, and helper scripts for strings, imports, YARA, capa, signal ranking, and hypothesis generation.

## Multi-Phase Workflow

The orchestrator skill collects and organizes analysis artifacts, highlights relevant signals, generates evidence-backed hypotheses, builds a basic component model, and prepares a prioritized deep-analysis plan. The process automates tasks such as fingerprinting, string extraction, API analysis, signal ranking, hypothesis generation, and component modeling.

## Repository Location

The project is available at https://github.com/mrphrazer/agentic-malware-analysis and is ready for use with Claude Code and Codex CLI.

Based on the search results, I found information about the Malaika paper:

## Malaika: Understanding Malware through Tri-Grounded Agentic Reasoning

The paper is titled "Malaika: Understanding Malware through Tri-Grounded Agentic Reasoning" by Xingzhi Qian and 3 other authors (Xinran Zheng, Yiling He, and Lorenzo Cavallaro), and it was submitted on July 10, 2026.

**Key Contribution:**

Malaika is a multi-agent framework that operationalizes three grounding mechanisms through analyst-inspired reasoning, tool-mediated evidence localization, and retrieval-based behavioral attribution. The framework is instantiated for Android malware analysis and evaluated on malware-understanding tasks.

**The Three Grounding Mechanisms:**

The paper argues that three complementary forms of grounding make malware analysis reliable: domain grounding (an analyst-style loop of hypothesis and review), semantics grounding (tools that localize execution-relevant program context), and knowledge grounding (external threat knowledge retrieved only after concrete behavior is seen).

**Significance:**

The findings suggest that grounding-aware reasoning provides a principled foundation for reliable malware understanding and, more broadly, for evidence-grounded software analysis.

The paper appears to have been published on arXiv (arXiv:2607.09179) and addresses a key challenge in malware analysis: reconstructing malicious behaviors from sparse, dispersed code evidence.

## Overview

Open-ReverseLab is an open-source AI reverse-engineering agent platform and MCP server for Ghidra, Frida, x64dbg and Rizin — automated PE/APK/binary analysis, CTF and malware research, with 100+ MCP tools and a 194-article runnable knowledge base.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about the jadx-ai-mcp project:

## Overview

JADX-AI-MCP is a plugin for the JADX decompiler that integrates directly with Model Context Protocol (MCP) to provide live reverse engineering support with LLMs like Claude.

## How It Works

The project is a Java plugin for the JADX decompiler plus a companion Python MCP server that lets LLMs like Claude inspect and analyse Android APKs in real-time, exposing rich reverse-engineering tools over Model Context Protocol so an LLM can locate vulnerabilities, review code and decompile resources on-demand.

## Key Components

JADX MCP Server is a standalone Python server that interacts with a JADX-AI-MCP plugin via MCP (Model Context Protocol), letting LLMs communicate with the decompiled Android app context live.

## Available Tools

Available MCP tools include fetch_current_class(), get_selected_text(), get_all_classes(), get_class_source(), get_method_by_name(), search_method_by_name(), search_classes_by_keyword(), get_methods_of_class(), get_fields_of_class(), and get_smali_of_class().

## Author

The project was created by Jafar Pathan (zinja-coder), a working professional in the field of Cyber Security.

The project is actively maintained on GitHub at https://github.com/zinja-coder/jadx-ai-mcp and licensed under Apache License 2.0.

Based on the search results, here's comprehensive information about Microsoft Project Ire:

## Overview

Microsoft has unveiled Project Ire, an autonomous AI agent capable of reverse engineering and classifying malware at an unprecedented scale. Project Ire automates what is considered the gold standard in malware classification: fully reverse engineering a software file without any clues about its origin or purpose.

## How It Works

The AI model can reverse engineer suspect software files and use forensic tools such as decompilers and binary analysis to deconstruct the code in order to determine whether the file is hostile or safe. The evaluation process starts with automated reverse engineering to determine the file type and structure, then the system reconstructs the software's control flow graph using frameworks like angr and Ghidra, and performs iterative analysis of each function with the help of language models and specialized tools.

## Performance Results

The breakthrough system achieved a precision rate of 0.98 and a recall of 0.83 during testing on Windows drivers, marking a significant advancement in cybersecurity automation. Tested on a dataset of known malicious and benign Windows drivers, Project Ire has correctly identified the nature of 90% of all files, and flagged only 2% of benign files as threats.

## Key Features

For each file it analyzes, Project Ire generates a report that includes an evidence section, summaries of all examined code functions, and other technical artifacts. This transparency enables security teams to audit and validate the system's findings.

## Deployment

Microsoft wants to deploy Project Ire as an internal analysis tool within the Defender ecosystem under the name Binary Analyzer.

# Malaika: Understanding Malware through Tri-Grounded Agentic Reasoning

## Architecture

Malaika implements a multi-agent framework operationalizing three complementary grounding mechanisms:

1. **Domain Grounding**: Constrains hypothesis generation and evaluation for malicious behaviors
2. **Semantics Grounding**: Localizes and connects supporting program evidence
3. **Knowledge Grounding**: Supports behavioral attribution through externally verifiable threat knowledge

The system uses "analyst-inspired reasoning, tool-mediated evidence localization, and retrieval-based behavioral attribution."

## Tools & Methodology

The framework integrates tool-mediated approaches to identify security-relevant signals within Android malware. It combines static analysis capabilities with LLM-based reasoning to reconstruct high-level malicious behaviors despite partial observability.

## Evaluation

**Domain**: Android malware analysis tasks

**Key Findings**:
- Outperforms prior LLM-based malware-analysis frameworks
- Demonstrates that reliability depends on both model capability and reasoning process
- Shows grounding-aware reasoning produces more precise and auditable conclusions compared to frontier agentic frameworks

**Metrics**: Emphasis on analysis quality, precision, and auditability of conclusions

## Code Availability

The document provided does not indicate code or data availability details.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about the OWASP Top 10 for Agentic Applications 2026, specifically regarding ASI01 (Agent Goal Hijack) and ASI02 (Tool Misuse):

## OWASP Top 10 for Agentic Applications 2026

The OWASP Top 10 for Agentic Applications 2026 is a peer-reviewed security risk framework for autonomous AI agents, published by the OWASP GenAI Security Project on 9 December 2025.

### ASI01: Agent Goal Hijack

Agent goal hijack happens when an attacker redirects an agent's objective or decision path through content the agent reads, rather than through code the agent runs. The agent still believes it is pursuing the user's goal. It is actually pursuing the attacker's.

The defining incident is EchoLeak (CVE-2025-32711, CVSS 9.3), the first known zero-click attack on an AI agent. A single crafted email planted hidden instructions that Microsoft 365 Copilot later retrieved as context.

### ASI02: Tool Misuse and Exploitation

Tool misuse and exploitation involves misusing legitimate tools through unsafe chaining, ambiguous instructions, or manipulated tool outputs.

### Key Principle

The OWASP concept of "Least Agency" is the architectural principle behind the whole framework: only grant agents the minimum autonomy required for safe, bounded tasks.

# Project Ire's Analysis Process

## Chain of Evidence
Project Ire maintains "a detailed, auditable trail that shows how the system reached its conclusion." This traceable log documents each analytical step and supports secondary review by security teams, allowing investigators to understand the reasoning behind classifications.

## Validator Tool
The system employs a specialized validator that "cross-checks claims in the report against the chain of evidence." This tool leverages expert statements from malware reverse engineers on the Project Ire team to verify findings and flag unsupported assertions during analysis.

## Claim Verification
When the system cannot fully validate claims, it marks them as unsupported rather than including unverified statements in final reports. The article describes an instance where Project Ire misidentified anti-debugging behavior; the validator flagged this assertion, and the issue was later resolved through updated decompiler rules.

## Evaluation Performance

**Public Dataset (Windows drivers):**
- Precision: 0.98
- Recall: 0.83
- Correctly identified 90% of all files
- 2% false positive rate

**Real-world Hard-Target Files (~4,000 samples):**
- Precision: 0.89
- Recall: 0.26
- 4% false positive rate

## Report Contents
Generated reports include evidence sections, summaries of examined code functions, and technical artifacts documenting identified malicious behaviors like process manipulation, network communication, and code injection techniques.

# JURIG Summary

**What It Does**
JURIG is an autonomous AI-powered reverse-engineering framework with a terminal UI. It analyzes Android APKs, DEX files, and native binaries through an agentic loop that decompiles, inspects, and instruments targets using dynamic analysis.

**Tool List (25 total)**
Recon: manifest parsing, URL extraction, secret scanning, native library enumeration. Static analysis: jadx, apktool, radare2/ghidra, ELF/PE parsing, hexdump, strings. Dynamic: adb, Frida (process listing, scripting, presets for SSL unpinning/class dumping), MITM proxy. Filesystem: read/write files, directory listing, shell execution.

**Model Providers**
Anthropic, OpenRouter, Claude CLI (subscription), Ollama (local), Kimi, Moonshot, Qwen/DashScope, and Cursor (subscription via native auth). Users select providers/models interactively in the TUI or via `JURIG_PROVIDER` environment variable.

**Sandboxing/Isolation**
No explicit sandboxing mentioned. Tools run as native subprocesses in the user's environment; dynamic analysis uses device-based Frida instrumentation without explicit isolation guarantees.

**Evidence/Reporting**
Markdown reports rendered inline with syntax-highlighted code previews. Session data (conversation, prompt history) persists to `session.json` per target for reproducibility.

**Tests/Evals**
No test suite or evaluation results documented.

**Release Maturity**
18 GitHub stars, 3 forks, 13 commits. No formal releases published; status lists MVP as operational.

**License**
Not specified in provided content.
